---

> **⚙️ STANDING CONVENTION — D-121: EVERY QUESTION AND RECOMMENDATION LEADS WITH TWO NAMED LENSES (Joe, locked 2026-07-19).** Before any option list reaches Joe and before any recommendation is made, state **① user-visible impact PER OPTION** — what does a person using XGen see, feel, or come to believe? — then **② resource cost** (build time, bundle size, maintenance and test surface, future work created). Architectural elegance, symmetry and implementation convenience are **tertiary**: still recorded, never leading. **⚠️ ADDITIONAL LENSES, NOT A REPLACEMENT** — D-065, D6, N-091, D-110/D-111, D-067, the no-anonymity core and GDPR discipline stand **undiminished**; D-121 adds a pass, it does not mint a trump card. **⚠️ A COLLISION GOES TO JOE UNRESOLVED, NAMED AS A COLLISION** — never silently traded, above all where identity, the wire, or anything irreversible is touched: *a rule that can be outranked by an appeal to user experience is not a rule, and the trades this project exists to refuse are exactly the ones that feel good to a user in the moment.* **⚠️ "NO USER-FACING IMPACT" IS A LEGAL AND EXPECTED ANSWER** — say it plainly and decide on cost; *a manufactured UX rationale is worse than none, because it launders an internal preference as a user's interest.* **THE REASON GOVERNS THE APPLICATION:** Joe locks architecture but cannot independently verify most technical claims put to him — these two axes are the ones he CAN judge, so stating them is what makes a recommendation **checkable rather than trusted blind**. **🔑 THE EVIDENCE (J-559):** Chat recommended the D2 composer read the selection bus on a sound architectural argument; **Joe asked what it would do to the user's experience**, and reading `stream-panel.svelte` showed **R5 latches the room** — so the composer would have **greyed out while the user was still looking at the conversation**, on ordinary navigation, unexplained. **The recommendation flipped.** *The architecture argument was not wrong; it answered a question that ranks below the one that decides.* **PHASE-0 CONSEQUENCE:** the user-visible consequence must be **GROUNDED, not reasoned** — read the code, run the probe, say what the user sees; *a principle that says "decide by user impact" without a step that MEASURES it merely relocates the guessing.* **CLAUDE'S HONEST BOUNDARY:** resource cost is **measurable** and must be measured rather than estimated; **user experience is only ever INFERRED** — Claude never observes a user, so on many such calls **Joe is the better judge**, which is exactly why they are stated for him instead of acted on silently.

> **⚙️ STANDING CONVENTION — LETTER SUFFIXES ON DESIGNATIONS MEAN TWO DIFFERENT THINGS, AND THE BARE NUMBER IS THE DISCRIMINATOR (Joe, 2026-07-26; Chat's formalisation of existing practice — ⚠️ CONFIRM OR AMEND).** Applies to every designation family alike: `J-` · `D-` · `N-` · `M-`.
> **① COLLISION SPLIT — the bare designation is RETIRED.** Where one designation was issued twice for two unrelated things, **the original takes `a` and the newer takes `b`**, and the bare number ceases to exist. **Already practised SEVEN times and never written down until now:** `D-030a/b · D-031a/b · D-037a/b · D-038a/b · D-039a/b · D-055a/b · D-056a/b` — in every case there is no bare heading, and the two halves are unrelated (`D-030a` *node packaged as a system service* vs `D-030b` *runtime file placement*).
> **② ADDENDUM — the bare designation SURVIVES.** A follow-on that extends or corrects an existing record takes the next free letter while the original keeps its bare number: `N-124` + `N-124a` + `N-124b`; `M-RP2.30` + `M-RP2.30a` (*the `fill?` amendment*); `M-RP2.31` + `M-RP2.31a`.
> 🔑 **THE DISCRIMINATOR NEEDS NO NEW NOTATION: ASK WHETHER THE BARE NUMBER STILL EXISTS.** Present ⇒ the letters are addenda. Absent ⇒ the letters are a collision split. **This holds across all three families with no exceptions**, so both conventions can coexist unambiguously — but only if a collision split actually retires the bare number, which is therefore **normative, not cosmetic**.
> ⚠️ **NEVER resolve a collision by renumbering one side to a free number.** Every existing citation of the old number would then silently point at the wrong record — and citations are how this project's chronicle is navigated at all.

> **⚙️ STANDING CONVENTION — NO BACKTICKS IN ANY POWERSHELL GIVEN TO JOE (locked 2026-07-17).** Joe hates backtick (`` ` ``) line-continuation — a stray trailing space silently breaks it, and it is not a real character for this purpose. When Claude (Chat **or** Clair) prepares a commit/push or any PS block for Joe: put each command (each `git …`) on **ONE physical line** — multiple `-m` flags on the same line — **or** use a here-string (`@" … "@`, opener at line-end, closing `"@` at column 0) for long multi-paragraph commit bodies. **No backtick line-continuation, ever.** (Git workflow otherwise unchanged: explicit `git add <file>` per file, never `git add .`; `git status` before commit; Joe pushes.)

Joe, 2026-07-26: *"we need label to mark state just before actual documents' editing, for any occasion, especially if we would have to make fallback."* **`git reset --hard roadtree-pre-legc` returns the whole tree here.** 📌 **It is also Leg C's V7 anchor** — ⚠️ **the runbook originally cited a bare commit hash, which had ALREADY gone stale one commit later**; a tag is placed at the state you mean and stays there, and **a hash pasted into a document does not.**

> **🎨 APPEARANCE MODEL — the CSS layer stack is CANONICAL (J-504/J-505, `ui/docs/xgen-css-layer-model.md` v1.1). READ IT BEFORE TOUCHING ANY GLYPH OR SKIN RULE.** The *appearance* sibling of `xgen-region-dock-model.md` (which owns *layout*). **The stack, Joe-locked:** `theme-*.css` (custom skin, the override layer) → **`skin.css` + `glyphs.generated.css` — ONE layer, the default skin, split by WHO WRITES IT** (hand vs generated) → the normalizes (**reset, not skin**). **A theme overrides a glyph exactly the way it overrides a colour — the cascade IS the mechanism.** **D-108: a glyph is a SKIN TOKEN — `core` owns the NAME (content), the skin owns the SHAPE (geometry).** A component never writes geometry, for the same reason it never writes a colour (N-025/N-090). **CDP-proven:** CSS **`d:`** works in WebView2 · **`var()` resolves inside a custom-property value** → **ONE** skin rule (`.icon path { d: var(--g) }`) serves every icon · a later stylesheet **redraws any glyph with zero component change**. **D-109: the Chromium/WebView2 `d:` dependency is DELIBERATE and NOT hedged** — the WebKit exit is the `-url` form the bank already emits.

> **🔒 D-110 — THE SPACE-THEME BAN. A TRUST BOUNDARY, NOT A STYLE RULE. Ch6 §6.2 REWRITTEN + §6.3.1/§6.3.2 NEW (Ch6 v0.5).** **A SPACE MAY RE-COLOUR. A SPACE MAY NOT RE-DRAW, AND MAY NOT RE-LAYOUT.** Ch6 §6.3's Layer 3 is a theme **declared by a Space OWNER and delivered over the wire** — unrestricted, it would let a Space owner **redraw a lock, a warning, a verified mark, or the AI badge (§6.13)**: icon spoofing in an identity-verified protocol. Colour (incl. the glyph **tint**) ✅ · **geometry (`--glyph-*`, `--glyph-*-url`) ❌** · layout/metrics ❌ · **everything not on the allowlist ❌ by default** (allowlist, never denylist). **🔑 TWO NORMATIVE CONSEQUENCES, both binding on future code:** **(a) A KEY ALLOWLIST ALONE IS THEATRE** — a Space theme is a key→value **map**, but a client that **string-concatenates a stylesheet** lets a malicious **VALUE** escape its declaration and inject arbitrary CSS. **MANDATORY: apply via `element.style.setProperty()` (CSSOM cannot break out) + validate with `CSS.supports()` + scope to the Space subtree — ALL THREE; any one alone is insufficient.** **NEVER interpolate a wire value into a `<style>` text node.** **(b) THE BAN BINDS THE GENERATOR BACK** — **`--glyph-*-url` MUST be emitted COLOUR-FREE** (a `currentColor` mask), because a data-URI with colour baked in **fuses colour and geometry into ONE token** → the seven `%23e6e6e6` glyph re-emits are now a **SECURITY REQUIREMENT**, not a tidy-up. *(Lesson worth keeping: **an access rule is only real if the data model can express the distinction it draws.**)* **Locked BEFORE implementation — `state.space_theme` is in NO Rust, NO TS, NO Svelte. `theme-*.css` DOES NOT EXIST. No milestone may claim theming works, and none may ship a Layer-3 applier that does not implement Ch6 §6.3.2 in full.** **Ch6 §6.2's four-layer model (D-057/D-058) is superseded IN PART** — intent survives (minimal reset, 13px/1.35, 4px unit); **`tokens.css` never existed, and component `<style>` blocks are FORBIDDEN, not required** — *the rule that makes skinning TOTAL is the rule that forbids the component from participating in it.* **Still open:** the exact colour-token allowlist · user-disable of Space themes.  

> **🔒 D-111 — A CLIENT MUST NOT FETCH A HOST CHOSEN BY SOMEONE ELSE (J-506, Ch2 v1.3, N-103).** **⚠️ OPENS WITH A RETRACTION — J-504/J-505 listed *"`url()` fetches"* as an open trust surface. IT IS NOT ONE.** Under **D-110's own** colour-only allowlist, `CSS.supports('color','url(…)')` is **false** — a `url()` **cannot enter a Space theme**. Fonts are **bundled** (Ch6 §6.2). *A threat named from pattern-matching, in the same doc that forecloses it — the SECOND overclaim in two turns on one topic. Retracted, not hedged.* **🔑 THE REAL INVARIANT, and the protocol had ALREADY WON IT:** *a client that fetches a URL chosen by **someone else** is a **BEACON** — it leaks the reader's **IP** and **read-time** to a host of the **sender's** choosing. **XGen publishes your XGID by design; it does NOT publish your network location.*** **Every avenue is already shut, structurally:** `message.image`/`message.file` carry **`xgen://hash/sha256:<64-hex>` — a CONTENT ADDRESS, NOT A LOCATION** (`xgen-core/src/blob_store.rs`; federation-native M12; per-blob client-encrypted; store *"content-blind by construction"*) · **no HTTP client in any crate** · fonts bundled · themes URL-free. **🔑 A HASH CANNOT NAME A HOST — the beacon is not *blocked*, it is UNSAYABLE.** *Content-addressing was taken for integrity and bought privacy for free; nobody had written it down.* **⚠️ THE ONE ROW THAT INVITES IT BACK: `link previews`** (Ch2's *"Client decisions — implementation freedom"* table). Unbuilt — but a client fetching preview URLs from message text hands the **sender** every reader's IP + read-time, invisibly, on every message. **✅ RULE: link previews — and ANY rendering that resolves an outbound URL — are fetched NODE-SIDE, NEVER client-side.** The Node already talks to the world; the Client deliberately does not. One fetch per link, not one per reader. *Freedom preserved: **whether** and **how** to preview stay the client's; only the fetch **LOCATION** is fixed — because it is not a rendering decision, it is **a privacy boundary wearing a rendering decision's clothes**.* **⚠️ WHAT ACTUALLY REMAINS, AND IT DWARFS EVERY GLYPH AND TOKEN: D-036 MODULE WIDGETS** — third-party HTML in an isolated webview; **CSP/sandboxing still OPEN at Ch6 §6.8.8** (filed Session 2, untouched since). **Unlike blobs, themes and glyphs, it has NO structural foreclosure at all.** Ch6's, not the UI track's. **Filed, not solved.** *Standing lesson: **a threat list padded with a foreclosed item is worse than a short one** — it trains the reader to skim, and the real entry gets skimmed with it. **Retract, don't hedge.***  

> **✅ D-112 + D-113 — THE PLUGIN TAXONOMY CLOSED AND THE MODULE-UI SANDBOX FLOOR LOCKED (J-507, Ch6 v0.6, `docs/xgen-plugin-taxonomy-phase0.md`).** **Locked together — you cannot classify a thing while leaving open what it is allowed to do.** **🔑 THE FRAME WAS ALREADY IN THE CODE:** `xgen-common/src/module.rs` (SE-D2) already says *"one unified handshake mechanism; the code term `kind` carries the system/ui distinction — a **module** is a *system* plugin (`host = node`), a **plugin** is a *ui* plugin (`host = client`)"*, plus **UUIDv4 slot/impl GUIDs that never federate** and *"the descriptor is a **const in the plugin's own code** — there is **no manifest file**; metadata is authoritative, location is never trusted."* *(Honest limit: the vocabulary is shipped; the `host`/`kind` FIELDS are not — `Descriptor` is `{kind_id, impl_id, name, assurance}`.)* **D-112 — ONE plugin, THREE axes:** **`host`** (node = system · client = ui) · **`delivery`** (`compiled` · `service` · `packaged`) · **`surface`** (`none`·`region`·`shelf`·`window`, at most one). ***"Module" and "widget" are not two species.*** **The drift was never Ch6-vs-D-102 — it was §6.8 vs EVERYTHING THAT WAS BUILT** (§6.8 predates the spine, the Auth registry, the widget tier, the region model and `WidgetMount`; **§6.8 moved**). **🔑 THE SLOT INVENTORY IS NOT A RIVAL — WE ALREADY BUILT IT:** half regions, half **content anchors inside a host widget**, and `message.details: WidgetMount[]` + unknown-id drop (W-13) **is** `room.message.decorator` **under another name** → **ONE placement model + ONE containment model. A slot is declared by the HOST, never requested by the guest.** **A THIRD species neither spec listed:** the **Auth Module is `delivery: service`** (`AuthModuleXgid` + `endpoint_url` + revocation — Ch6 §6.8.7 **corrected**). **Settings = `surface: window`; NO `screen` kind** (Discord full-overlay foreclosed knowingly — reversible on one word). **🔒 D-113 — THE BOUNDARY IS *DELIVERY*, NOT "WIDGET":** `self-panel` needs no CSP; **being `packaged` is what is dangerous** — the question sat on the wrong noun for three months. **S-1: a packaged module UI is a webview with NO NETWORK** (only its own local IPC channel) → **D-111's beacon is UNSAYABLE inside a module** · S-2 packaged assets · S-3 own origin, **no Tauri IPC** · **S-4 the module NEVER holds the key** (consent ≠ key handover; **our Rust signs, per event**) · **S-5 no trust chrome** (never the identity/verified/**AI-badge** zone — D-110 generalised) · S-6 deny-by-default caps · **S-7 no `packaged` plugin loads until S-1…S-6 ship** (**zero lines exist today → the lock costs nothing and forecloses everything**). **CLOSES Ch6 §6.8.8's widget-sandboxing question (open since April 2026)** + *module signing* + *module permissions*. **⚠️ NAMED, NOT SOLVED: the `compiled` LOADER is still open (`temperature.rs`) — a DYNAMIC LIBRARY IS NOT A SANDBOX; > **🔒 M-RP6.1k — DESIGN LOCKED 2026-07-12 (J-510 → D-114 + D-115). Runbook `tasks/M_RP6_1K_UISTATE_STORE.md` handed to Clair. 🟢 PLAY.** **🔑 TWO SPECS DISAGREED ABOUT WHO PERSISTS THE LAYOUT, AND GROUNDING — NOT RE-DERIVATION — SETTLED IT.** region-dock §9 specified a **layout-only** store (`xgen-client_layout.json` → `xgen-client_layouts.json`, five verbs, auto at **M-RP7.3** / named at **M-RP7.6**); surfaces §4 specified a **UI-state** store. **They were never two objects — §9 is simply the EARLIER, NARROWER DRAFT**, written before window geometry, the shelf, or a named-workspace concept existed. **🔒 D-114: ONE store — `xgen-client_uistate.json`.** §9's identity/reconcile rules **survive verbatim** as the `layout` key's own (`widgetId` durable · drop unknown · re-inject `system` · `version`+migrate); its **two filenames, its five verbs, and its “the layout manager is itself a widget” line are SUPERSEDED** (region-dock **v1.8**). Auto-save/load **and** named states are **both pulled forward from 7.3/7.6 into 6.1k** — S-8 already locked manual **named** states as *permanent, not a stopgap*, and the shelf faces dispatch them. **🔑 THE CARVE-OUT — AND IT IS THE REAL CONTENT OF THE LOCK: §4's inherited *“Rust persists an OPAQUE blob, never learns the shape”* rule CANNOT hold uniformly, and §4.2 is what breaks it.** The monitor-work-area **clamp is MANDATORY**, and **only Rust can read a work area, or apply a rect before the webview exists** (apply it later and the window visibly jumps). ***A clamp Rust cannot perform is not a clamp.*** → **`geometry` = a TYPED Rust struct** (`x,y,w,h,maximized`) **· every other key = an opaque `serde_json::Value`, round-tripped verbatim.** **This is J-499's rule APPLIED, not excepted:** J-499 killed `get_layout` because Rust would have **duplicated a type the webview owns** — **geometry is the OPPOSITE case**, a type **Rust already owns and the webview cannot**. ***Rust owns what only Rust can do, and stays blind to what the webview owns.*** *(A rule stated once and then applied without asking which side of it each thing falls on is how a rule becomes a superstition.)* Free consequence: an opaque value **preserves unknown keys** → every future key is additive with **zero Rust change**. **⚠️ AND THE FINDING THAT SHAPED THE MILESTONE — GREPPED, NOT ASSUMED: FIVE OF THE SIX §4.5 KEYS HAVE NO LIVE SOURCE TODAY.** Only **window geometry** has a feeder (resize grip + native decorations — the user moves it every session). **grid layout** = a **const** (`DEFAULT_LAYOUT`; no user mutation until renderer B) · **shelf favourites** = waiting on ④ pinning (the top strip mounts empty) · **collapsed/expanded** = **ZERO `collapsible` props anywhere in `ui/client`** (the 8 leaves are non-collapsible `section`s) · **theme** = `theme-*.css` **DOES NOT EXIST** (D-110) · **last open room** = no room selection until **M-RP6.2**. → **6.1k SHIPS `geometry` + `layout` ONLY, AND RESERVES NOTHING.** An empty `theme: null` key is the ***`tabs`-branch / N-091 shape at file scale*** — **a key nothing writes is a key nobody has round-tripped**; each remaining key lands **with the milestone that creates its source**. *(§4.5 is honoured, not narrowed: §4.5 locked what BELONGS in a UI state; **D-065 governs WHEN each one is built**.)* **🔒 D-115 — M-RP-WINSTATE's own written criterion FIRES → B** (own it; **`tauri-plugin-window-state` NOT taken**; M-RP-WINSTATE → **⬛ SUPERSEDED, absorbed**). **Unit = PHYSICAL px** (N-092b, measured twice) — ***a rect can only be compared to a monitor work area in the same unit, so a logical rect makes the mandatory clamp unimplementable***. **Clamp-don't-refuse EXERCISED** (write an off-screen rect, launch, watch it centre). **⚠️ AND N-095's DoD MOVES FROM M-RP7.3 TO 6.1k:** its deferral's premise was that `loadLayout()` **cannot return null** — and **6.1k is the milestone that kills that premise** (it is where `loadLayout()` stops returning a constant and starts parsing a real file). Leaving it at 7.3 would mean **7.3 closing a hole 6.1k opened**. ***A deferral is valid only as long as its premise holds; when the premise dies, the deferral dies with it — it does not quietly inherit a new one.*** **RENAMED before the commands ever existed (2 lines): `layout.save`/`layout.load` → `uistate.save`/`uistate.load`** — **the store is NOT a layout** (it holds geometry, and will hold shelf/theme/room); `layout.*` would be a lie by 6.2. **No `saveAs` id** — one diskette, one dialog, two outcomes. **LEGS — VISIBLE FIRST (Joe's standing brief: see the UI early, correct it while the milestone is open):** **A** save + load **dialogs** (shell-local, the `about-dialog` precedent) + **both commands enter `commandTable`** + **`diskette`/`load` FLIP ENABLED**, against an in-memory store → *the milestone's UI is on screen and correctable before one line of plumbing exists* · **B** Rust `get_ui_state`/`set_ui_state` + the file + the `loadLayout()` body swap + **N-095 EXERCISED** · **C** geometry save (`CloseRequested` + debounced move/resize) + restore + **clamp EXERCISED** · **D** named states carry geometry (§4.2) + session key + CDP verify → close. **Real client 9222 ONLY** — no sampler cells; **the sampler catalogue must stay 328, and that is a verify leg**. **`cargo test` MUST MOVE from 1507/0/62** — the honest signal that Rust landed (the inverse of the 6.1i/6.1j *“identical to baseline PROVES no-Rust”* leg). **Client registry baseline: 46, read QUIESCENT (N-105).**

Then Legs B/C/D shipped persistence and nobody swept it** — so the milestone reached close-out with a store that survives a relaunch **while the app told the user their workspace was not being saved.** Caught in the **painted DOM** at Rule-5 re-drive, with two named states sitting on disk to prove the contradiction rather than argue it. ***A STALE HONESTY NOTE IS STILL A FALSE STATEMENT — and it is WORSE than a missing one, because it was written by someone being careful, so the next reader trusts it.*** **The discipline already existed and was not generalised:** 6.1j bound *“the disabled face is a COUNTDOWN, not a resting state”* — **a W-8 disclosure is the same object.** The face got swept **because the DoD named it**; the note got missed **because nothing named it**. → **RULE: no milestone closes leaving its own UI asserting a phase-limit it has since removed. Cost of the fix: **4 lines**. It was the cheapest defect in the milestone and **the only one that would have reached a human**.)* **🔒 D-114 realised LITERALLY:** `get_ui_state -> String` / `set_ui_state(json: String)` — a **raw-string opaque round-trip**; **ZERO descriptor types in the Rust crate** (the only `widgetId` in `xgen-client` is a raw string *inside a test*, which is the **proof of opacity, not a breach**); `WindowGeometry {x,y,width,height,maximized}` is the **only** typed part. ***Rust owns what only Rust can do, and stays blind to what the webview owns.*** **🔒 D-115 realised:** physical px throughout; clamp-don't-refuse **exercised** (off-screen rect → centred); **N-095 relocated here and exercised** (corrupt store → `DEFAULT_LAYOUT`, region-shell present, **no blank centre** — the J-499 30→21 failure **proven absent**); **`tauri-plugin-window-state` NOT taken**. **RESERVE NOTHING held — and it is visible on disk:** top-level keys are **exactly** `version`/`session`/`named`/`active`. No `theme`, no `shelf`, no `collapsed`, no `room`. **D-101 untouched** (config only) — the store is the project's **first deliberately persistent user-facing state**, by design. **🔑 N-108 — THE REGISTRY BREATHES WITH *STORE CONTENTS*, NOT JUST MENU STATE (extends N-105):** the Load dialog's picker/Load/Delete sit inside `{#if entries.length}` → quiescent count is **55 empty-store** and **59 with a saved state**. ***A baseline that depends on a DATA FILE is a baseline that can be wrong on a machine where nothing is wrong*** — **assert what is IN THE STORE, not merely that the popups are shut.** *(J-509's phantom one layer down.)* **Measured as a TRANSITION in ONE session — 55 → save → 59 → delete → 55**, exact return, zero leaks; a number **derived** by arithmetic was **refused entry to the record until it had been seen**. **🔑 N-107 — TWO WRITERS, ONE FILE:** Rust merges `session.geometry`, the frontend merges `named`/`active` — **read-modify-write on BOTH sides**, neither serialising a whole-file model it does not own. ***Any format with more than one writer must be MERGED, never REPLACED*** — whole-file serialisation by a narrow writer is **data loss wearing a save button's clothes**. Proven on disk: emptying `named` left `session.geometry` **byte-intact**. **MEASURED (Chat re-drove every leg, Rule 5 — not one number taken on report):** client registry **55** quiescent empty-store (`count===unique`) → **59** with a state · **`cargo test` 1517/0/62** (from 1507, **+10**, summed programmatically not hand-counted) · `vite build` **165** · `npm test` **41** · sampler catalogue **328 UNCHANGED** (by scope: zero `ui/core`/`ui/sampler` across both commits) · `:modal` true (on `:modal`, never the `open` attribute) · split ratios `[1,2,7,2]` **exact at a THIRD distinct width** · unknown key **survived the app's own write-back** · zero component-local `<style>`, accent-neutral. **Two legs in NOBODY's matrix, run at verify:** (1) **loading a named state with NO `geometry` key** — the disk had *grown* one (`001`, saved before Leg C existed; **real data outran the fixtures inside one session**) → window **unmoved**, centre alive, **both guards held** (`if (s?.layout)` / `if (s?.geometry)` — the J-499 blank-centre trap **designed around and cited by name** before it was ever hit); (2) **Delete is TWO-STEP** — the button re-labels to **“Confirm delete”** (**S-6's *destruction is deliberate* honoured inside the dialog**, and never in the DoD). **And “static gates need the apps down” stopped being folklore:** `cargo test` with the client up dies in 15 s on `failed to remove file …xgen-client.exe` — **the running app holds the binary**. **Deviations (Rule 6, flagged not absorbed):** shell-local `.svelte.ts` store (**the better call** — the exact seam Leg B swapped into) · comboboxes on both dialogs (Joe, mid-flight) · autofocus focus-catcher (so `showModal()` doesn't pop the combobox) · **the core `dialog` footer suppressed via `:has()`** → its `__close` stays **mounted-but-hidden and still registers** → **FILED, NOT BUILT: a footer snippet slot on the `dialog` core** (**correctly kept out** — a `core` change inside a shell milestone is what makes a registry delta unreadable; its own milestone) · **`quit`-command geometry save — a genuine find made IN VERIFICATION:** `app.exit` (Ctrl+Q / File→Exit) does **not** reliably fire `CloseRequested`, so the final rect would have been **silently lost on that path**. *Found because someone drove the app, not because someone read the code.* **+N-107/N-108/N-109; > **✅ M-RP6.1l CLOSED (J-513) — THE PLUGIN LIST SHIPS. Commit `1dc5849` (6 files, +272/−17). 🟢 NEXT-ACTIVE = M-RP6.2 — R1 Spaces + R2 Rooms on real `KnownSpace`. BACK TO THE GRID.** **The gear is enabled; NO shelf face in the app is disabled** — the 6.1j countdown is discharged. **Shipped: the FIRST plugin registry in the project** (`ui/common/lib/plugins/registry.ts` — **D-112's three axes `host`·`delivery`·`surface` in code for the first time**), with **`widgetRegistry` DERIVING from it** (*a widget is in the grid **because** it is a registered plugin with `surface: region`*), and a **read-only pane** (5th widget, `kind: system`, `surface: none`) in a shell-local modal. **Three honest rows, `[system]` badge, no faked verbs.** The grounding held: **there was no registry to enumerate, so the milestone created the first one** — it did not project one out of Rust (J-499's rule; the inverse of D-114's geometry). **⚠️ CORRECTION 1 — A COUNTDOWN NAMES *WHO* FLIPS A FACE, NEVER *WHEN*. THIS MILESTONE WAS BUILT TOO EARLY AND THE RECORD IS WHY.** 6.1j wrote *“no face is enabled before its command exists, **and no milestone closes leaving its own face disabled** — 6.1k flips diskette/load, 6.1l flips gear.”* **Those are TWO rules wearing one sentence.** The first is a real guard (**never enable a control that resolves to nothing**). The second is a **SCHEDULE**, smuggled in beside it unexamined — and it **pulled a milestone forward because a face was waiting, not because the product needed it.** Joe: *“I honestly thought we would grey the widget manager till we have a working grid.”* **He was right; the schedule was Chat's.** → ***A disabled face with a NAMED OWNER is honest indefinitely. A countdown names WHO discharges it — never WHEN, because that turns a guard into a deadline and lets the shelf drive the roadmap.*** *(Kept, because it is not nothing: the derive is the seam every future region drops into, and it is verified. The sequencing that produced it is not something to be pleased about.)* **🔒 CORRECTION 2 — J-512's D6 IS SUPERSEDED (Joe). A GREYED BUTTON IS NOT ONE THING.** D6 refused Remove/Disable/Launch/Settings outright (*“no verb → ships ABSENT, not faked”*) — **too blunt: it collapsed two controls that look identical and are opposites.** **Grey because the verb was never built = a DEAD CONTROL** (it lies by implying the capability exists). **Grey because the plugin's OWN DESCRIPTOR says so = W-13, RENDERED** — *a disabled **Remove** on a `[system]` row **is the information**, and Ch6 §6.8.5 drew exactly that.* → **THE RULE: every button's state is DERIVED FROM THE DESCRIPTOR, never hardcoded, and a control is disabled > **🔒 THE DOCK ENGINE (RENDERER B) — PHASE-0 LOCKED 2026-07-12. `docs/xgen-dock-engine-phase0.md`. THE CHAPTER THE WHOLE M-RP7 ARC RESTS ON — READ IT IN FULL BEFORE PROPOSING ANYTHING IN THIS ARC.** Renderer A (`region-shell`, M-RP6.1f) renders a **const** tree into flex boxes and **nothing mutates it** — *it looks like a dock and is a picture of one*. Renderer B turns it into one: tile frame, fold, splitter resize, drag-to-rearrange. **It is a RENDERER UPGRADE, not a region rewrite** — both renderers read one D-103 descriptor. **🔑 §0 — JOE'S AUTONOMY GRANT: Chat holds full authority over every mechanical aspect of this arc — descriptor, mutation algebra, gesture semantics, persistence, verification, milestone split — EXCEPT THE GRAPHICAL APPEARANCE OF THE CREATED ELEMENTS, WHICH IS JOE'S.** And per **N-090 appearance is WIDE**: gaps, spacing, sizing, tracks and layout are skin-tunable, so **stripe height, grip size, seam thickness, band thickness, the folded form and the hole raster are all Joe's, not Chat's.** *The grant is over mechanics, not over the record — findings, deviations and gaps are surfaced as always (D-065).*  

> **🔒 D-116 — THE DOCK REARRANGES; IT NEVER JOINS. LOCKED (Joe, 2026-07-13).** **A TARGET TILE IS AN ADDRESS, NOT A CONTAINER.** In a space-filling tree there is no empty space to drop *into* — the tree's only vocabulary is **relative**: *above that one · below · left · right*. **The drag verb is not "put A into B"; it is "insert A here, and *here* is expressed as an EDGE of B".** The target receives nothing; it is a **lookup key**. → **NO centre drop-zone** (the centre of a tile is **inert**) · **NO tabs, ever produced** · **NO docked/undocked mode** (a mode was only ever needed to discriminate split-vs-tab; and *a region is docked iff it appears in the layout tree* — **a query, not a flag**; two sources of truth for "is this docked" is a D-067 drift surface) · **NO `M-RP-ROVING` prerequisite** (a tab strip would have been roving's **5th** independent impl; it is not built, so the extraction stays filed and stays out of this arc). **Fold IS the stacking mechanism and it is the better one:** four folded stripes in a column is a tab strip lying on its side — except **several can be open at once** and **every label is always visible**. ***Tabs is what you reach for when you don't have fold. We have fold.*** **The door stays SHUT, not LOCKED:** `types.ts` keeps typing `tabs`, `resolve.ts` keeps dropping it with a DEV warn — zero cost, zero schema change if it is ever wanted. **⚠️ If a future milestone reaches for tabs it RE-OPENS §3 EXPLICITLY — it does not arrive as a rider on a drag milestone.** **⚠️ AND D-116 IS NOT WEAKENED BY HOLES (J-514/J-515):** §2's *"there is no empty space to drop into"* is now only *mostly* true — but **D-116's ground is Joe's own constraint (*"never mixing or joining"*), NOT the geometry.** The rhetoric was corrected; the decision stands untouched.  

> **✅ M-RP7.1 — THE TILE FRAME: STRIPE, GRIP, FOLD — CLOSED (J-514). Commit `4c2f886` (12 files, +433/−93).** `region-tile` = the **35th `core`** — frames every leaf: title **stripe** `[move-grip · title · fold]` · **body slot** (now the scroller, ex-`.region-leaf`) · the **reused `status-bar` SE clip-path triangle**. **The eight `Section` roots are UNWRAPPED** — no widget draws its own title (Joe's *"group containers will be contained, but not as a main form"*, landed as a concrete edit in eight files). `collapsed?: boolean` on the leaf; `version: 2`; **migrate a no-op.** Move grip + resize triangle are **PAINTED AND DEAD** (`aria-hidden`, no handler, no role, no cursor — they carry no claim that later becomes false); **dischargers named: move → M-RP7.4 — drag to dock · resize → M-RP7.2 — splitter resize on the seam.** **🔑 THE FINDING — FOLD OPENED A HOLE, AND THE CHAPTER HAD ALREADY PROMISED IT COULDN'T.** Phase-0 §4.1 (one session old) says *"No hole is ever created"*; **that is true about the CROSS axis and silent about the MAIN one.** Fold every child of a split and the split **under-fills — a hole opens.** **It is in the shipped build, and Joe found it by looking at the screen.** *(The argument that produced §4.1 reasoned about a single tile — and **a single tile cannot see its siblings**. A proof about one node is not a proof about the tree. Fifth in one family: N-091 · N-097 · N-099 · N-109 · this. **A verified claim is only ever as wide as the case that was actually run.**)* → **new milestone: M-RP7.1b.** **⚠️ D-117 WAS DRAFTED AND DELIBERATELY NOT LOCKED** — Joe superseded it before it reached `DECISIONS.md`, and his word on the replacement was ***"honestly i have to see it in practice."*** **→ no fold decision enters the record until it is built and looked at.** *A `D` locked for a design its author says he needs to see first is a prediction wearing a decision's clothes.* **MEASURED (Chat re-drove every leg, Rule 5):** client registry **67 — quiescent, EMPTY store, NO SELECTION** · **⚠️ N-108 EXTENDED — THE REGISTRY BREATHES WITH THE *SELECTION* STATE TOO: 67 (none) → 71 (selection) → 65 (selection + self folded). A baseline that does not state its SELECTION state is unreadable** → every future baseline states **store state AND selection state** · `cargo test` **1517/0/62 IDENTICAL** (*proves* no Rust) · `npm test` **43** · `vite build` **168** · sampler catalogue **328** · ratios `[1,2,7,2]` exact at **1242px and 1252px**. **⚠️ FILED, NOT FIXED — A LIBRARY-WIDE DEFECT: `mergeClasses` DOES NOT DEDUPE**, and `envelope` already stamps the type-class from `name` (N-023) → **any component that ALSO writes a literal `class="X"` renders `class="X X"`.** Five registered elements do it today: `region-shell` · `self-panel` · `inspector-panel` · `combobox` · `plugin-list`. **Clair fixed `region-tile` only — correctly, that was her scope.** The sweep (or making `mergeClasses` dedupe — a **`$common` base change**) is **ITS OWN MILESTONE, NEVER A RIDER.** **TOOLING, measured not folklore:** ⚠️ **the CDP bridge WRAPS getters** — `get(id)` returns `{type, state}`; read **`get(id).state.foo`** (reading `get(id).foo` returns **all nulls, which looks exactly like a broken build**) · tile classes are `region-tile-stripe`/`-move`/`-title`/`-fold`/`-body`/`-resize` (**not** `-strip`/`-grip`) · ⚠️ **an attribute selector with an unquoted `#` throws a bare `EVAL ERROR`** (quote it, or iterate `querySelectorAll('[data-debug-id]')`) · escaped double-quotes inside `-Expression` break the harness (use single quotes in the JS, or `String.fromCharCode(39)`) · `npm test` lives in **`ui/sampler`**, `vite build` in **`ui/client`** · ⚠️ **`cargo test` EXCEEDS THE MCP TIMEOUT — run it DETACHED and poll the PID in SEPARATE SHORT CALLS**; a long `Start-Sleep` kills the shell **and takes the detached run with it**, leaving a truncated log that read **1195/0/60 — plausible, complete-looking and WRONG**, betrayed only by the **missing final `test result:` line**. ***A killed detached run leaves a MEASUREMENT-SHAPED ARTIFACT.***  

> **✅ M-RP7.1b — THE FOLD AXIS BECOMES THE USER'S CHOICE; SPLITS SHRINK-WRAP; THE HOLE GETS A FLOOR. CLOSED (J-516).** Design locked J-515; shipped `0f25e50` + `14eb4d8` [Clair] + the appearance fix [Chat]. Phase-0 → **v1.4**. **⚠️ STILL NO FOLD `D` — Joe has now SEEN it run and has not asked for the lock. D-116 stands alone.** Runbook `tasks/M_RP7_1B_FOLD_AXIS.md` (COMPLETED). **Zero Rust · zero sampler · zero node**, verified by diffstat. **🔑 THE FINDING WAS A SCREENSHOT.** M-RP7.1's own arc table said its purpose was *"this is where Joe sees it and corrects the appearance"* — **he did, and it corrected more than the appearance.** He folded two regions, saw the empty band, and raised three things; **they turned out to be one thing.** **⚠️ The holes are NOT caused by the new design — they are IN THE SHIPPED BUILD** (N-111). **① THE FOLD AXIS IS THE USER'S CHOICE — TWO BUTTONS:** `[<]` folds left (collapses **width** → vertical strip) · `[v]` folds top (collapses **height** → horizontal stripe) · **folded ⇒ the unused button is DISABLED, the used one unfolds.** **Two axes, NOT four directions.** *(Cost, honestly: §4.1's elegant free property — "drag a folded tile col→row and it re-orients itself" — **DIES**. **The right trade: it was elegant for the TREE and surprising for the PERSON.** And it finishes a sentence §4.1 only half-wrote — it made fold **available** everywhere and left the **direction** an accident of the tree.)* **`collapsed` stops being a flag and becomes a DIRECTION (`'width' | 'height'`) — STORED, because it is user intent, and user intent is not derivable from anything.** The **along/across mode** stays **derived** (a fact about the tree). **② 🔒 A SPLIT SHRINK-WRAPS WHEN ALL ITS CHILDREN FOLD ACROSS IT (§4.4) — the part Joe did not ask for, and the part that actually solves his screenshot.** **Fold is a LEAF verb; a split's width is a SPLIT property living in the PARENT's `sizes[]` — a leaf verb cannot reach a split property**, so the 2/12 column stayed 2/12 wide no matter how its children folded. **❌ `collapsed` on a SPLIT node was REJECTED on Joe's own lock:** a leaf takes its title from `CLIENT_PLUGINS.name` and **a split has NO NAME** → name field + naming UI + **chrome on every split, nested, forever** = ***a group container promoted back to a MAIN FORM***, the exact thing §5 killed. **✅ Instead:** all children folded **across** ⇒ the split shrink-wraps, **the weight returns to its siblings by the `flex` they already have**, unfold one child ⇒ it re-inflates, **weights never mutated**. **Costs NOTHING NEW:** no descriptor field (**derived** — a stored flag goes stale the instant a child unfolds, **D-067 in miniature, twice in one arc**), no split chrome, and the mechanic is **byte-for-byte the folded leaf's** (`flex: 0 0 auto`). **⚠️ Mixed folds (`[<]`+`[v]`) deliberately DO NOT shrink-wrap** — column stays wide, hole opens, raster explains it. ***No magic, no guessing what they meant.*** **③ 🔒 HOLES ARE LEGAL, PAINTED, INERT (§4.5).** **§7.1's *"no holes, rectangles only"* is AMENDED by the man who accepted it 24 h earlier, in the open.** The hole is **flex leftover space inside a split, not an element** → **the raster is a BACKGROUND on the split container: zero new DOM, one skin rule**, shipped **provisional** (*you cannot tune a raster under holes you have not seen*). **🔒 AND THE LOCK FOR M-RP7.4: A HOLE IS NOT A DROP TARGET — D-116 says a target tile is an ADDRESS, and a hole has NO ADDRESS.** *(Drop on the EDGE of the tile above it. **Allow drops into holes and we have quietly built free 2-D placement and retired the tree.**)* **⚠️ D-116 IS NOT WEAKENED — its ground is Joe's constraint, not the geometry. Correct the rhetoric; do not touch the decision.** **⚠️ NO `D` LOCKED, ON JOE'S WORD: *"honestly i have to see it in practice."*** D-117 is dead without ever having been written, **and the replacement is NOT being locked either.** Phase-0 v1.3 carries the design — *that is what a Phase-0 is for*; `DECISIONS.md` carries **what Joe has decided, and he has told us he has not.** ***A `D` locked for a design its author says he needs to see first is a prediction wearing a decision's clothes.*** **🔑 TWO GROUNDED FINDS THAT CHANGED THE RUNBOOK BEFORE IT WAS WRITTEN:** **① `migrate` DOES NOT EXIST** — grepped: the word appears **only in comments** (`types.ts:16-17`, `layout-default.ts:63`); **`version` has been bumped TWICE and there has never been a migrate FUNCTION.** → **7.1b CREATES it.** *A path described in three documents and implemented in none is not a path; it is a plan.* **② ⚠️ A LAYOUT CAN ALREADY BE ON DISK, and the Phase-0 said the opposite BY ACCIDENT** — §12's *"nothing writes `session.layout`"* is **true**, and it was **read as "nothing writes a layout"**, which is **false**: **`app_client.svelte:227` has persisted layouts inside NAMED UI STATES since M-RP6.1k.** Measured on disk: `named: {}` → **migration is free TODAY — BY LUCK, NOT BY DESIGN; one diskette click ends that.** → **the answer is not "don't click the diskette", it is WRITE THE REAL MIGRATE** (`v2→v3`: each `collapsed: true` leaf takes its parent's `dir` → explicit direction — the old derived rule made honest, ~10 lines) **— DoD: EXERCISED in vitest against hand-built `v2` trees under BOTH parent kinds. Fed, not asserted (N-091, applied to the one branch in this codebase that has NEVER ONCE RUN).** **🔒 §4.1 AND §4.4 SHIP TOGETHER, NEVER APART — two buttons without the shrink-wrap is a thin strip beside a huge hole, STRICTLY WORSE than today.** ***Shipping the disease and the cure a week apart is how a bad appearance gets defended.***

> **🔑 M-RP7.1b — WHAT THE CLOSE ADDED ON TOP OF THE DESIGN (J-516). READ THIS BEFORE THE NEXT LEG.** **① THE MIGRATE RAN — FOR THE FIRST TIME EVER.** Grounded before the runbook: **`migrate` DID NOT EXIST** — the word was **only in comments**; `version` had been bumped **twice** with **no function at all**. *A path described in three documents and implemented in none is not a path; it is a plan.* **`migrateLayout` is now real**, six vitest cases fed with hand-built `v2` trees — **and Chat DROVE IT LIVE** through the **real Load dialog** (plant a `v2` named state → shelf face → combobox → Load → `handleUistateLoad`). **Clair's hand-back said the live leg was not drivable; it was — the way in was three clicks, not a new harness.** *A branch you cannot reach through the product is a branch you have not tested.* **Two properties only the live run showed:** a migrated `v2` layout is **HOLE-FREE BY CONSTRUCTION** (the old derived rule only ever folded ALONG → **nobody's saved workspace can come back with a hole in it**), and **the migrate NEVER REWRITES THE STORED STATE** — a **read-path transform**; the record stays `v2` on disk and re-migrates every load. **Idempotent, non-destructive, never silently edits a file the user wrote.** **② ⚠️ THE DEVIATION WAS CORRECT AND THE RUNBOOK WAS WRONG.** Clair flagged (Rule 6) `migrateLayout(raw, **fallback**)` instead of the runbook's bare `migrateLayout(raw)` → **`resolve.ts` is `core` and `DEFAULT_LAYOUT` is SHELL-LOCAL; B would have pointed `>`, at the content). **Only the HEIGHT button spoke disclosure** (`v` = *"my body is below"*) — straight from Joe's original `[<][V]` sketch, **which mixes the two conventions in one stripe** — **and the skin comment directly above the rotations already described convention A correctly.** ***The label was wrong, the prose was right, and one rotation value contradicted both.*** **🔒 THE RULE: THE CHEVRON ALWAYS POINTS WHERE THE REGION WILL GO IF YOU PRESS IT** — enabled or disabled, folded or not; the **disabled** button keeps its open glyph, still truthfully naming what it WOULD do. **The fix was TWO ROTATION VALUES SWAPPED.** → **N-116: re-reading the chapter would NEVER have caught this — the CHAPTER was self-consistent and the CODE was not.** **④ `--region-fold-gap: 0` is a KNOB, not a reservation — and ONLY because of `display: inline-flex`.** On the shipped `display: inline` a `gap` is **INERT**, and **an inert knob IS a reservation** (§4.3.1 — RESERVE NOTHING). **The test: can Joe change the value TODAY and see the render move? Yes.** **⑤ 🔑 A FOURTH REGISTRY AXIS, AND IT LIVES ON DISK (N-115): ONE SAVED UI STATE = +4** (`textfield#uistate-load-pick__input` · `combobox#uistate-load-pick` · `button#uistate-load-go` · `button#uistate-load-del` — **element-absent when there is nothing to pick**, which is CORRECT per J-500). **→ `67` IS ONLY TRUE WITH ZERO SAVED UI STATES. One diskette click and the client reads 71 FOREVER** — N-108's shape, except **the "data file" is one the USER writes with a button.** **⚠️ AND `71` IS NOW AMBIGUOUS: selection-active = 71; zero-selection + one-saved-state = 71. SAME NUMBER, TWO CAUSES.** **Axes: quiescence (N-105) · store (N-108) · selection (N-112) · named-state count (N-115).** **⚠️ One anomaly FILED, NOT EXPLAINED (Joe: *"leave it right now"*):** one launch rested at **71** with Joe's **real identity** auto-selected; three later fresh launches all rested at **67, no selection**. `self-panel` selects on `onActivate` (a click), never on mount — **something activated it and Chat could not establish what. > ### 🔑 **N-120 — THE ONE DEFECT M-RP7.2 SHIPPED. CLAIR FILED IT "LATENT, OUT OF SCOPE"; CHAT REACHED IT; IT INVERTS A RESIZE. → A REQUIRED LEG OF M-RP7.3, NOT A FILED ITEM.**
> **`region-node` threads `path` from the RESOLVED child index; `resizeSplit` walks the DESCRIPTOR by that path** — and **`resolve.ts` DROPS** (unknown leaves · `tabs` · all-dropped splits). **Any drop and the two index spaces diverge.** **REACHED, NOT ARGUED:** plant ONE unknown `widgetId` → descriptor has 3 children, **2 tiles paint, 1 seam** (between `spaces` and `rooms`) → **drag it RIGHT to ENLARGE `spaces`** → descriptor `[1,1,1]` → **`[1660,340,1000]`**: **`spaces` HALVED (660→335px) and `rooms` GREW.** ***The resize did the exact opposite of the gesture*** — the pair resized was `ghost ↔ spaces`. **And 55% of the row's weight now belongs to a widget that does not exist** — which **M-RP7.5 would write to DISK**, and which would **reappear eating half the screen** if that widget returned. **🔒 THE ASYMMETRY, PROVEN IN THE SAME POISONED LAYOUT: FOLD IS DROP-SAFE (it addresses by `regionId` — an IDENTITY); RESIZE IS DROP-FRAGILE (it addresses by `path` — a POSITION).** ***A position is only an address in the tree it was counted in.*** **🔑 THE RULE, BIGGER THAN THIS ARC: a DERIVED VIEW MAY RENUMBER — an index into a derived view is NOT an address into the source.** Either the resolved node carries its **source index**, or every mutation addresses by **identity**. *`resolve.ts` already knows the descriptor index at the moment it drops — it just throws it away.* **⚠️ Unreachable in today's build** (all 8 ids registered, `tabs` never produced — D-116) — **reachable the first time a widget id is RETIRED OR RENAMED and a user loads a saved workspace** (the W-13 reconcile case; M-RP7.1b proved the Load dialog is three clicks away). ***"Unreachable today" is the argument that has now been wrong FIVE times here*** (N-091 · N-097 · N-099 · N-109 · N-116). **→ M-RP7.3 OPENS WITH IT: addressing IS algebra, and a misaddressed `resize` nudges two integers while a misaddressed `move` RELOCATES A PANEL INTO THE WRONG BRANCH.**  

> **🧪 `cdp-debug.ps1` GAINS `-KeepSelection` (J-519) — because A HARNESS THAT CAN ONLY PASS IS A WEAK HARNESS.** The harness was clearing the selection before every gesture, so it **AVOIDED** N-118 instead of **TESTING** it. **CONTROL (clear suppressed): two drags on the tile body → `mousedown, mousemove, dragstart` — AND THEN NOTHING. No `mouseup`.** **N-118 reproduced ON THE WIRE, with the native drag finally VISIBLE** (at J-518 it was only *inferred*). **SUBJECT: the seam, same conditions, a live 21-char selection → both consecutive drags COMMITTED, no `dragstart`, `mousedown: false`** — because **`preventDefault()` on `pointerdown` SUPPRESSES the compat mouse events** and the pointer events carry the drag. **Measured, not assumed.**

> ### ✅ **JOE'S REGION-OWNED GAP MODEL — SHIPPED at M-RP7.2b (J-523). ⚠️ READ THE M-RP7.2b BLOCK BELOW: it shipped, but the boundary it was locked to fix (tile↔hole) turned out to be INVISIBLE — N-124. The "dependency" on `M-RP-PLATE` was already retracted (J-521).**

The gap has to be **5px (not 10px)** between both of regions and also between region and edge."* **⚠️ That is TRUE MARGIN COLLAPSING and FLEXBOX DOES NOT COLLAPSE MARGINS — they ADD.** **But the result is exactly reachable: every TILE carries a full margin `G`; splits carry NOTHING; the seam becomes a ZERO-WIDTH flex child with `−G/2` margin each side, which cancels the double.** → tile↔tile **G** · tile↔edge **G** (***`--region-pad` DISAPPEARS — the edge gap BECOMES the region's own margin; that is literally the model***) · tile↔**hole** **G** (**today ZERO**) · **nested COMPOSES AT ANY DEPTH** because a split contributes nothing · **and the seam's zero-width box lands DEAD CENTRE, so the drag survives** (*a `gap` never was hit-testable; that is why M-RP7.2 had to build a seam element*). **⚠️ J-520 CLAIMED THIS WAS GATED ON `M-RP-PLATE` — WRONG, AND RETRACTED.** The model needs the splits **not to PAINT**, which is a **two-line skin move** (J-521 — the backdrop moved to `.region-shell`), **NOT the plate widget.** *Chat conflated "splits must not paint" with "the milestone that makes splits not paint".* **🔒 THE BLOCKER IS GONE: it is a small component change (tile `margin: G` · seam zero-width with `−G/2` · delete `--region-pad`), and it can land before or after M-RP7.3 — Joe's call.**

MEASURED:** the **split** painted `--s5` + the raster; **`.region-shell` AND `.app-center` were BOTH transparent.** → ***the gap BETWEEN regions showed the GRID's backdrop, and the gap AROUND the grid showed THE ROOM BEHIND THE GRID.*** **Two different surfaces for one conceptual gap.** *Joe's phrase — "the edge gap is made from outside" — described the implementation exactly, from the picture alone.* **🔒 NOW: the SHELL paints · the SPLITS are TRANSPARENT · the TILES are OPAQUE ⇒ every gap (seam · hole · perimeter) is the SAME ONE SURFACE.** **🔒 AND THIS IS ALREADY THE `M-RP-PLATE` ARCHITECTURE, arrived at early and for free** — that milestone now only swaps a `background-image` for a real element. **⚠️ `.region-split` MUST NEVER GET A BACKGROUND AGAIN** (it would re-split the surface *and* block the gap model). **⚠️ NEAR-MISS: a two-hunk `Filesystem:edit_file` failed on its SECOND hunk, so NEITHER applied — while a separate edit removing the old background DID.** For one build **nothing painted the grid at all.** ***edit_file is all-or-nothing PER CALL, not per hunk — and the CDP read caught it, not the diff. A diff that looks applied is not a render.***

> **⏸️ `M-RP-RESTART` — FILE ▸ RESTART **AND** REVERT. FILED (Joe, 2026-07-14; J-520). LANDS WITH M-RP7.5.** Joe asked for a File ▸ Restart item, *"disabled in states where it cannot be executed — no problem for me."* **The guard is right; grounding it split the item in TWO.** **🔑 `Restart`** (bounce the process) is **real today** — **`tauri-plugin-process` is ALREADY in `xgen-client/Cargo.toml`** and that plugin *is* `restart()`/`exit()`. **⚠️ BUT IT IS DECLARED AND NEVER WIRED** — no init in any `xgen-client/src/*.rs`, no frontend use: **a dependency nothing consumes.** **⚠️ `Revert`** (*"no automatic save on exit, load last automatic save"*) **— THERE IS NO AUTOMATIC SAVE: the grid does not write `session.layout` until M-RP7.5.** → **"restart without saving" and "restart" are the SAME THING today, and `Revert` would be greyed 100% of the time — not "in some moments".** ***A permanently-disabled item is not a guard; it is a promise*** — the painted-dead chrome this project keeps refusing (J-500 · 6.1j), and **the identical reason M-RP7.6's grid lock cannot ship early.** **→ ship both LIVE, together, with M-RP7.5** — the milestone that creates the state they discard. **⚠️ Named now: after M-RP6.3 (the send verb) a restart tears down a live WS that may hold UNSENT MESSAGES.** *Restart gets more expensive the more the app actually does.*  

> **🎨 THE REGION'S OWN EDGE — `--region-border: 1px` · `--region-edge: var(--s4)` (Joe, 2026-07-14; J-522). Joe: *"give each region 1px very fine smooth border. When gap is 0, there is no sense of regions, all is melted."* He tested the extremes (0 and 20px) and settled on `--region-gap: 4px`.** **⚠️ THE COLOUR COULD NOT BE `--s5` ANY MORE — J-521 MADE `--s5` THE BACKDROP**, so an `--s5` edge is **invisible at any gap > 0**. The edge must read against **both** neighbours: darker than the backdrop (`--s5 #343b47`), lighter than the tile body (`--s #16181c`). **`--s4 #2a2f38` does both.** *A token that was correct for a hairline stopped being correct the moment the surface behind it changed.* **🔑 N-121 — AND A REAL `border` BROKE THE ARC'S BASELINE. MEASURED FIRST: `[1,2,7,2]` came back `[1, 1.97, 6.90, 1.97]`.** With `flex: n 1 0` flex distributes the **FREE** space by weight, and then each item's **BORDER BOX = content + 2×border** — a **constant** added to every tile. **Content boxes stay proportional; border boxes do not — and EVERY rect JS can measure is a border box.** **⚠️ WORSE THAN COSMETIC: the splitter computes its drag fraction from the neighbours' `getBoundingClientRect()` widths, so a border would have biased the resize arithmetic by `2×border` per tile — systematically, silently, forever, and nothing would ever fail.** **✅ FIX: `outline` + `outline-offset: -1px`** — identical hairline, **ZERO layout impact**; **re-measured `[1,2,7,2]` EXACT**, `--region-min` still 22, folded strip still 22px with **zero overflow**, clamp still stops at 22 without folding. *(An inset `box-shadow` is also layout-free but paints BELOW the children — the stripe's `--s2` would cover the top hairline. An outline paints ABOVE. ⚠️ Tiles are not focusable today; if one ever needs a focus ring it must NOT be an outline on this element.)*  

> **🔑 N-122 — AND JOE'S OWN EXTREME TEST HAD A HOLE HE COULD NOT SEE.** He set `--region-gap: 0`, saw it render, and reported *"the system withstands it"*. **It renders — but the SEAM ELEMENT *IS* `--region-gap` WIDE, so at 0 the seam is ZERO PX**, and `--region-seam-hit` was a **constant 1px** → a **2px grab target: alive, and ungrabbable by a human.** *He tested that it did not break; he did not test that he could still drag it.* **✅ THE HIT AREA NOW COMPENSATES FOR THE GAP:** `max(1px, calc((8px - var(--region-gap)) / 2))` → **a CONSTANT grab zone at every setting. MEASURED (`elementFromPoint` swept at each value): 9px @ gap 4 · 9px @ gap 0 · 22px @ gap 20 — and THE DRAG COMMITS AT GAP 0** (MID `[1,2,7,2]` untouched, AFTER `[180,120,700,200]`). ***A knob that silently kills a gesture at one end of its range is not a knob.*** **⚠️ `calc()` IS SAFE HERE ONLY BECAUSE THIS TOKEN IS NEVER READ BY JS** — `getComputedStyle` does **not** resolve `calc()` in a custom property; it returns the raw token stream and `parseFloat` gives **`NaN`**. **`--region-min` and `--region-snap` ARE read by JS and must stay PLAIN VALUES.**  

> **⚠️ N-123 — AND THE INSTRUMENT THEN BROKE JOE'S TOKEN, AND HE REPORTED IT AS A BUG IN HIS OWN CSS.** Joe: *"something happens, `--region-gap: 4px` doesn't work anymore."* **It was not his CSS.** Proving the drag at gap 0 (N-122) needed the token set in **one** CDP call and the drag in the **next**, so the override could not be undone inside a single eval — it went on as an **inline style** on `.region-shell` **and was never removed.** **⚠️ VITE'S HMR HOT-PATCHES CSS WITHOUT A RELOAD, SO THE INLINE OVERRIDE SURVIVED EVERY EDIT HE MADE** — inline beats the stylesheet; his 4px was applied and then silently overridden, indefinitely, **by a leftover from a test he never ran.** **🔒 RULES (in `CDP_DEBUG_HARNESS.md`): a probe that persists a mutation across calls OWES A CLEANUP CALL, and the cleanup is PART OF THE PROBE · any session that touched inline styles ends with `location.reload()` · prefer reloading between token variants over inline overrides.** ***N-118 said the instrument can lie to ME. N-123 says it can lie to JOE — and he has no way to tell that the thing on his screen is not the thing in the file.***  

> ### ✅ **M-RP7.2b — THE REGION-OWNED GAP MODEL — CLOSED (J-523). `skin.css` ONLY: no component, no descriptor, no vitest, no Rust.**
> **`--region-gap: 4px` is the ONLY spacing token. `--region-pad` and `--region-seam` are DELETED — not renamed, gone.** Every **tile** carries `margin: G` · every **split** carries **nothing** (and must never paint again — J-521's rule is now load-bearing: a nested split's box **overlaps its neighbour's margin**) · every **seam** is a **ZERO-WIDTH** flex child with `−G/2` each side, cancelling the double, **its box dead centre of the gap** so the drag survives. **`--region-seam-hit` re-derived → `max(4px, calc(var(--region-gap) / 2))`** — N-122's premise is retired (it compensated for a seam that WAS `--region-gap` wide); the seam is now always zero-width, so **the `::before` IS the entire grab target**. Floors: **≥ 8px** (a finger) and **≥ the gap the user can see**.
>
> ### 🔑 **AND THE THING IT WAS LOCKED TO FIX DOES NOT EXIST (N-124). ⚠️ "tile↔hole is 0" IS NOT OBSERVABLE.**
> The claim sat in the skin, in Phase-0 §4.5.2, in this PLAY block, and in the milestone's own runbook — **which Chat wrote before measuring it.** **A/B (inject the OLD geometry live, fold a tile ACROSS to build a real hole): folded-tile insets 4/4 vs 4/4 · distance-to-hole 774.8 vs 774.8 · the full gap census 4 EVERYWHERE under BOTH.** **🔒 Since J-521 moved the backdrop to `.region-shell`, A HOLE AND A GAP ARE THE SAME SURFACE** — a tile "butting onto a hole" is indistinguishable from a tile with a gap, *because there is nothing on the far side of that gap to be separated from.* **ZERO VISUAL DELTA.** **FOURTH TIME, SAME SHAPE:** N-118 my *reasoning* · J-521 my *architecture* · N-116 the *record* · **N-124 the *justification*** — and a 🔒 icon changed nothing except how confidently it got repeated.
>
> **🔒 JOE SHIPPED IT ANYWAY, ON THE RIGHT REASON: IT DELETES A MECHANISM, NOT A TOKEN.** The perimeter was the *shell's padding*, the inter-tile gap the *seam's thickness* — **two mechanisms, two tokens, which had already drifted apart once** (J-517: 1px vs 6px; Joe caught it by looking). **They can never drift again, because they no longer exist.** And **a gap that lives on the REGION travels with it when M-RP7.4 moves it**; a gap that lives on the GRID exists only where the grid puts a seam.

> ### ⚠️ **N-124a — TWO BASELINE NUMBERS IN THIS ARC WERE MORE PRECISE THAN THEIR MEASUREMENT. USE THE NEW ONES.**
> **`[1,2,7,2]` WAS NEVER BIT-EXACT.** Recorded "EXACT" at four window widths; at full float precision it is **`[1, 2.000112, 7.000224, 2.000112]` — and the OLD geometry returns `2.000114`.** ~0.004px of **pre-existing Chromium sub-pixel, in BOTH models** — **which is exactly what proves the margin adds no bias** (N-121's border broke it by **1.5%**; this is **0.006%**). **And the clamp stops at `22.29px`, not "exactly 22"** (integer-weight rounding, L2; the OLD geometry rounds to **21.89 — just BELOW the floor**). ***A number you rounded before you recorded it is a number you can no longer use as a control.***
>
> **MEASURED (Chat re-drove every leg, Rule 5):** perimeter **4/4/4/4** · **14 adjacent tile pairs all exactly 4**, incl. pairs crossing a split boundary (**it composes at depth**) · **MID-drag** descriptor `[1,2,7,2]` untouched with the button still down, **AFTER** `[194,106,700,200]` (pair total 300 invariant, untouched siblings ×100) · grab zone **9px @ gap 0 · 9px @ gap 4 · 20px @ gap 20**, seam wins at x=113–115 *inside* the neighbour's border box (**N-119's `z-index` re-proven**) · registry **67** (the zero-width seam registers nothing) · `npm test` **59** · `vite build` **169** · zero Rust **by scope**.

> ### ✅ **M-RP7.4b — THE EXACT PREVIEW: REHEARSE THE DROP — CLOSED (J-527). `region-shell.svelte` ONLY. Zero Rust · zero sampler · no `mutate.ts`/`skin.css`/schema change · `cargo test` 1517/0/62 IDENTICAL · `npm test` 77 · `vite build` 169.** 7.4a drew half the target as it is NOW; `move` reflows the grid first (N-127), so it was up to 120px off. 7.4b computes the preview from a **DRY-RUN of `move`** (pure + total → **zero side-effect, V6-proven**: the live layout is byte-identical *while the preview shows*) + `resolveLayout` + a **weight-proportion walk that MIRRORS the renderer's own rules**: `flex: {weight} 1 0` → `weight/Σweights×axis`, with a folded-along leaf / shrink-wrapped split taking a fixed `--region-stripe` strip out of the pool (`carriesMainAxisWeight` **reused from resolve.ts, not re-derived** — not a second flex model, D2/N-126). **🔑 THE REFLOW IS FIXED — the whole point: `spaces`→`stream` right left went 60px → 2px, width 38px → 4px; the region previews in the RIGHT PLACE. The strip-exclusion works — V3 (drop into a column holding a folded strip) landed top 1px.** **⚠️ N-128, and I STOPPED to report it rather than claim the ≤2px the runbook demanded (Rule 3/5/6): the runbook's "within 2px" measured ONE split level's four column widths; the preview walks a MULTI-level path down to a TILE, and weight-math is exact for WEIGHTS but structurally blind to the fixed GAPS (each tile's 4px margin per side + the inter-tile gap at every level), which ACCUMULATE — measured left 2/width 4 on the reflow axis but top 7/height 14 on a 3-level wrap.** *Weight-proportional-from-a-dry-run is the SAME rule the renderer applies for weights, so it is not a second model — but it mirrors only the weight rule, not the gap rule; the gaps are a real floor that grows PER LEVEL, not a flat 2px.* Chasing them precisely = the §7 second-model trap; **§5 (mount the hypo tree offscreen, measure) is the truly-exact path and stays filed, unbuilt.** **Resolution (Joe): the bar is OPTICAL — *"we don't need super exact computations… if the highlighted rectangles are in optically correct positions and size, i am satisfied."*** The residual is ~1–4% of the rect, invisible in use → shipped, floor recorded as-is, **NOT "pixel-perfect."** Ground-truth verify: capture preview mid-drag → commit the real move → read the moved tile's **FRESH** rect (`matches:1` — after a move the tile is a NEW node, N-125) → restore. **`M-RP-PREVIEW-EXACT` ⬛ superseded.** Records: dock-engine Phase-0 **v2.3** · ui-notes **v0.92** (N-128 + N-127 correction: conditional 1–120px, not flat 40–100) · task COMPLETED · ROADMAP **v4.98**. **No new D.**

> ### ✅ **M-RP6.6 — the client resident: live connection + traffic accounting — CLOSED (J-543). Single-seat, Chat technical lane (Joe: "all technical stuff is yours; mine is visual"). All three legs live-verified on real client 9222 + real node; the ConnStats row-swap left to Ms Design (§7) — the milestone closes CODE-COMPLETE with the two `'N/A'` rows STILL STANDING (a swap outside the visual lane is how a provisional appearance decision gets made by accident). Code + records ready; Joe pushes.** The discard-the-stream scaffold (`connect_async`→drop→`sleep(150ms)`→READY — *"wired in 2b/M3"* since M1, **never was**) is now a REAL resident. Closes the LIVE half of gate F-1: *"stop the node → the led flips"* was un-runnable until now. **§0 open decision RULED (Chat, technical-autonomy grant): Path A.** N-116 cut the other way — Phase-0's byte seam (*"choke `send_bytes` / `recv()`"*) was **unusable under D3**: `send_bytes` is **private**, `recv()` returns a **parsed `Inbound`** (frame length dies inside core). So the resident cannot see bytes without editing GPL core → **a client-crate `CountingStream` interposed BELOW the WS handshake** (stream-layer counter; core untouched; scope = all socket bytes incl. handshake). **Shipped (7 files, client crate + frontend, ZERO xgen-core):** **D1 spine** `resident.rs` — `run_session` (connect→auth→drain) + injected `FnMut(ClientLifecycleState)` sink (UI `emit_state` / headless `tracing`, one spine two callers, D-056; `service.rs` refactored onto it = the pure-move regression-lock). **Leg A** real lifecycle from actual outcomes (node via `resolve_node`, real `client_authenticate`, no sleep). **Leg B** `run_resident` reconnect loop + pure unit-tested `Backoff` (`1,2,4,8,16`→30 cap; owns `Reconnecting`). **Leg C** `CountingStream`+`connect_counted`+resident-timed RTT (`ping`↔`Pong`, `want_ping` flag so ping never races the recv borrow) + `TrafficStats`/`get_conn_stats` (mirrors `get_pacing_state`) + `selfState.traffic` slot (`{bytes_in,bytes_out,rtt_ms}` snake-case verbatim, absent-not-zero: `rtt_ms:null` before first pong) + a 2 s `app_client` poll. **🔑 THE FINDING (→ N-137): a real bug only the live app could show.** First kill proved DETECTION worked (log: `recv error os 10054` → `session ended Disconnected`) **but the UI stayed READY** — `run_session`'s drop arms *returned* `Disconnected` without *emitting* `sink(Disconnected)`, while the connect/auth-fail arms did (silent divergence; `reached_ready` unit test asserted the mapping, not the emission — N-099 family). Fixed by making the **CALLER own the terminal state** (Leg A emits `Disconnected`, Leg B emits `Reconnecting`) — one owner, and exactly Leg B's shape. ***A return value is not a notification; returning and emitting are two acts.*** **VERIFIED (Rule 5, real-node session):** Leg A `CONNECTING→AUTHENTICATING→READY` (real auth) then kill→`DISCONNECTED` (CDP live). Leg B `RECONNECTING↔CONNECTING` gaps walked the schedule against WALL-CLOCK, then the **same process** (same log, no restart) auto-reconnected when the node returned. Leg C `__XGEN_SELF__.traffic` `{587,609,rtt 0}`→grew to `{595,633,0}` across a ping (live-cumulative; `rtt 0` = real sub-ms loopback, not the null sentinel — reached READY THROUGH the counted stream). **GATES (measured, N-105): `cargo test` 1524/0/62 = floor 1519 + the 5 new resident tests EXACTLY** (0 failed, final line present, no N-117 truncation) · `npm test` 77 · `vite build` clean · sampler catalogue 328 unchanged. **D3 (GPL core `Connection` UNTOUCHED) + D2 (live ingest OUT) confirmed AGAINST THE DIFF — 7 files, zero `xgen-core`; the drain still discards inbound.** **No new D** (D1–D5 arc-local; §0 = a D3-note revision). **No new `core`.** **OWED / HANDED OFF:** ConnStats **row-swap → Ms Design** (§7, absent-not-zero binding) · **live ingest** (R5 fan-out into the drain) → its own milestone (R5 + M-RP6.3). **🟢 NEXT-ACTIVE = M-RP6.3 — live messaging (composer/send verb) + the R5 stream wrap** (D5: the resident now owns the socket; send writes over it). `temperature-indicator` ⏸️ POSTPONED. → JOURNAL J-543 · `tasks/M_RP6_6_IMPL.md` COMPLETED · N-137 · ROADMAP v5.10.  

> ### 🟢 **M-RP6.3 — LIVE MESSAGING: R6 COMPOSER + NARROW-B SEND PATH — PHASE-0 LOCKED (J-544). Design-only, NO CODE. Phase-0 `tasks/M_RP6_3_COMPOSER.md` v1.0 ACTIVE. Six arc-local decisions D1–D6, by-recomm under Joe's autonomy grant. Joe pushes.** **AUTONOMY GRANT (Joe, this session): *"after we assert and agreed the form of socket, you have autonomy — except visual appearance."* Chat's own recorded boundary on top of it: appearance → Ms Design · the auth-tier question → JOE (it sits on the no-anonymity core) · wire-spec changes → Joe · pushes → Joe. Arc creation, leg splits, sequencing and invariant naming are Chat's.** **THE SEND PATH IS B (unify — outbound multiplexes over the resident's ONE socket), chosen on a USER argument and narrowed by grounding.** Chat's additions to the case: **under A most of M-RP6.6 would be DECORATIVE** — a client that only opens a socket to send has no sustained thing to be in a *state* about, so the led, the lifecycle, RECONNECTING and the traffic rows would all describe a fiction (D-065, not performance); plus **ordering** (one socket = one ordered stream; concurrent short connections race, and out-of-order chat reads as brokenness) and **offline send** (B + 6.6 backoff makes type-while-down-flush-on-recovery possible). **The honest half: the user never experiences architecture until it FAILS** — B adds a *silent* failure mode A lacked, which is why D6's binding rule exists. **🔑 GROUNDED @ `aa3cc3e` — SIX FINDINGS; THREE SHRANK THE MILESTONE, ONE CHANGED IT.** (1) **NO RETRY CAP EXISTS** — `run_resident` loops forever, `Backoff{attempt}` private/getter-less/saturating → **`(2/10)` is NOT EXPRESSIBLE today.** (2) **`next_attempt_at` is NOT PUBLISHED** (delay computed + slept inside `run_resident`) → **a countdown is NOT EXPRESSIBLE today.** (3) **The lifecycle sink cannot carry either** — `FnMut(ClientLifecycleState)` is a BARE enum, no payload → a 2nd published surface, mirroring the shipped `TrafficCounters`/`get_conn_stats` (one pattern, not a new mechanism, D-067). (4) **The detection window is ~10 s TWICE OVER** — `PING_INTERVAL` 10 s (dead peer unnoticed) + `CONNECT_TIMEOUT` 10 s (each dial) → **the countdown MUST hand off to `connecting…`**, or zero-then-ten-seconds-of-silence reads as FROZEN. (5) **⚠️ `send_event_confirmed` OWNS THE CONNECTION AND EATS THE DRAIN** (`connection.rs:186`, discard `_ => continue` at `:243`) — under a unified socket **fan-out events are SWALLOWED while a send awaits its ack**, directly incompatible with live ingest; and it is a **FAMILY** (`upload_blob:256`, `fetch_blob:323`, `batch.rs::get_dag_tips`), sweep **~114 client call sites** → *"move correlation into the drain" as a GENERAL rule is a crate-wide refactor.* (6) **NO AUTH TIERS, NO SESSION LIFETIME** — `auth.rs` is nonce→Ed25519→verify; `CHALLENGE_EXPIRY_SECS=30` bounds the CHALLENGE, not the session; no expiry/re-auth/scopes/roles anywhere; `ws://` only. **🔑 THE NARROWING — JOE'S THREE QUESTIONS (streaming · bulk · security) EACH INDEPENDENTLY ARGUED FOR IT.** **Streaming:** WebSocket has **NO multiplexing** (unlike HTTP/2 or QUIC) — head-of-line blocking is INHERENT to one socket; `BLOB_CHUNK_BYTES` chunking is the mitigation but only if the drain is fair, which loads D3 with more work than it was given. **Bulk:** `upload_blob`/`fetch_blob` chunk **base64 (+33%)** over the same WS **and own the drain** → on the resident socket a big upload leaves the client **BLIND TO FAN-OUT FOR THE WHOLE TRANSFER** (minutes), no cancel/resume/progress. **Security:** B does not create the auth weakness, it **extends its DURATION by orders of magnitude** (A: a stolen socket is worth seconds; B: as long as the app runs); MLS covers *content*, not metadata (who/when/how-much — which 6.6 Leg C now literally counts); M6 admin verbs on a shared socket would inherit **BYTE-IDENTICAL** auth to a chat message. **🔒 D1 — NARROW B (the ONE call NOT taken under the grant; Joe confirmed explicitly): the resident socket is the CONTROL/MESSAGE PLANE ONLY.** `ops::*` keep their own short-lived connections — already correct under **D-056** (control-mode, not resident). *B was decided for the user's MESSAGING experience; that is the composer, not `create_space`.* Same user-facing win, blast radius off the ~114 sites. **🔒 D2 — THREE PLANES, a STANDING INVARIANT:** control/message → the resident socket · bulk → own connection per transfer · real-time media → out of scope. **BINDING ON ALL FUTURE MILESTONES: the own-connection-and-drain family (`send_event_confirmed`, `upload_blob`, `fetch_blob`, `get_dag_tips`) may NEVER run on the resident socket.** **🔒 D3 — NON-BLOCKING DRAIN is a CONSTRAINT, not a style** (send queues + returns; the drain writes and correlates without stalling inbound); `ops::send` stays a **SOFTWARE** fallback, **never a NETWORK** fallback. **🔒 D4 — BOUNDED RETRY + TERMINAL STATE + AUTO-RESUME:** capped attempts (uncapped kills the counter AND the terminal state), terminal **`disconnected — [Reconnect]` as a REAL ACTION, not a greyed spinner**, auto-resume on window-focus/network-return (capped-without-resume kills the sleeping-laptop case) — *both halves required.* **🔒 D5 — SHAPE SETTABLE, DO NOT WIRE SETTABLE** (Joe: *"prediction in the code, that it has to count this possibility"*): named constants in ONE place, values travel to the UI as **published data** never hardcoded a 2nd time in Svelte, no behaviour keys on a specific number; **EXPLICIT NON-GOAL: no config-plumbing layer** — migration waits on **J-513**. *A named constant migrates in one line; a premature config layer charges now for a decision not yet made.* **🔒 D6 — FAIL-FAST DEFAULT + an EXPLICIT queue offer: BLOCK SEND, NEVER BLOCK TYPING** (killing the textarea mid-thought is the worst answer to a blip); a long outage **OFFERS** "send when reconnected", never a silent queue. **BINDING: A MESSAGE MAY NEVER *LOOK* SENT WHEN IT IS NOT.** *Honest limit: a HALF-OPEN socket means the first send after a drop can fail EVEN WITH THE GUARD IN PLACE — blocking sends is a nicety, never a guarantee; per-message failure state is still required.* **LEGS: A** resident status publication (Rust — cap, attempt count, next-attempt-time, terminal state, `[Reconnect]`, auto-resume) · **B** outbound multiplexer + `event_id → oneshot` correlation IN THE DRAIN (Rust; **live ingest — the M-RP6.6 deferral — WIRES HERE**, the drain becomes the single reader) · **C** R5 stream wrap + the **LIVE MUTABLE ITEM CLASS R5 DOES NOT HAVE TODAY** (R5 is append-only fixture-driven; the gap item counts `reconnecting (2/10) · retrying in 6…`, hands off to `connecting…`, and **COLLAPSES IN PLACE** to `connection restored · 8s` — *the live widget IS the historical marker, matured*; ~2 s grace so a recovered blip is NEVER SHOWN — *silence is the correct UI for a blip*) · **D** R6 composer — **a BUILD, not a wrap** (R6 does not exist). **⚠️ TWO NUMBERS WERE MERGED IN DISCUSSION AND SEPARATED IN DESIGN:** *"restored · 8s"* is **RETROSPECTIVE** (outage length, final on resolve); *"8…7…6…"* is **PROSPECTIVE** (time to next retry) — both ship, the 2nd is the better feature (it makes the backoff LEGIBLE) but **requires the scheduler to publish `next_attempt_at`, which it does not today.** **🔑 THE GAPS THE UNIFIED SOCKET SURFACED — FILED AS ARCS, NOT ABSORBED (Joe: *"we need to close gaps … certainly!"*). NONE BLOCKS M-RP6.3 SHIPPING** — the resident dials a LOCAL node today; what they block is **the resident talking to anything BEYOND LOOPBACK**, so **the gate is on DEPLOYMENT, not on the milestone** and the UI arc proceeds. §3 risks: **R-1** no TLS · **R-2** no session lifetime · **R-3** no auth scopes/tiers (**admin/user separation under D1 is mitigated BY CONVENTION, NOT BY MECHANISM** — recorded honestly) · **R-4** metadata exposure · **R-5** bulk has no cancel/resume/progress, +33% base64. **🔒 GATE: `wss://` IS A HARD PREREQUISITE BEFORE THE RESIDENT DIALS ANYTHING NON-LOOPBACK** — a gate, not a preference. **SEQUENCE: M-RP6.3 → M-SEC-TLS → M-SEC-AUTHSESS → M-BLOB-PLANE** (TLS first — cheapest, unblocks the most; auth-session before the blob plane so the blob plane inherits a SETTLED session model rather than being retrofitted twice). **⚠️ M-SEC-AUTHSESS IS NOT A TECHNICAL SCOPING CALL** — *"what is an identity authorized to do, and for how long"* sits on the **no-anonymity core**; **JOE LOCKS IT**, it may need spec/doc work before code, and it is flagged as possibly deserving its own grounding session before it is even scoped. **Chat explicitly DECLINED to lock it under the autonomy grant.** **OWED:** the M-RP6.6 ConnStats **row-swap** (Ms Design) · `tasks/M_RP6_6_RESIDENT.md` **v1.2** (§2 D3 still says *"resident-level wrapper"*; shipped code is the stream-layer `CountingStream`, and the *"auth handshake excluded"* caveat is **now WRONG** — the wrap happens BEFORE authenticate, so auth bytes ARE counted) · **all appearance in Legs C+D → Ms Design** (§5 specifies *what states exist, what they mean, when they appear* — no colour, type, spacing, glyph or animation). **NEXT-ACTIVE = M-RP6.3 Leg A. Entry (Rule 0): CLAUDE.md PLAY → JOURNAL J-544 → `tasks/M_RP6_3_COMPOSER.md`.** → JOURNAL J-544 · ROADMAP v5.11.

> ### ✅ **M-RP6.3 Leg B — OUTBOUND MULTIPLEXER + ACK CORRELATION + LIVE INGEST — CLOSED (J-546). 🔑 THE ARC RETURNED TO THE CHAT–CLAIR SPLIT: Chat built it and ran V1–V3, CLAIR VERIFIED V4–V6 — and found a defect in LEG A, already closed and pushed. here a live check asserted a value and never the transition that value is a claim about.* **A field is not verified by reading it in the state where every implementation agrees.** **Fix:** the rule is EXTRACTED as a named, unit-testable **`ResidentStatus::on_lifecycle(&state)`** (reaching `Ready` zeroes `attempt` + clears the countdown) and `run_resident` wraps the sink so EVERY transition passes through it — extraction is the point, the rule previously existed only as an implicit consequence of loop placement, which is why nothing could test it;
