an accessor edits GPL core, M-RP6.6 D3), so the `select!` gained an **outbound arm**: send enqueues and returns, the drain builds→signs→writes and correlates `event_id → oneshot` **without stalling inbound** (D3), reusing `TransportMessage::event_id()` + the shipped `EventConfirm` rather than minting a parallel outcome type (D-067) · a 1 s sweep resolves entries past `SEND_ACK_TIMEOUT` as `timed_out` · **teardown resolves every survivor to `failed` WITH A STATED REASON** (dropping the map would surface as a bare `RecvError` — an in-flight message must resolve to a *reason*, not to silence) · **`SendOutcome` is FOUR-WAY, never a boolean** (`accepted`/`rejected`+code/`timed_out`/`failed` — **folding `timed_out` into success or failure is exactly the D6 lie**) · live ingest → `xgen-event` → the new `$common` `ingest.svelte.ts` store, **verbatim, no projection** (the `MessageDescriptor` projection is R5's at Leg C; doing it here would put it in a second place before the first exists) · `service.rs` threaded with an **inert-by-construction** message plane (one spine, one code path). **🔑 B-5 — THE FINDING THAT RESHAPED THE LEG (Joe-locked option 2):** a message needs `prev_events`, every `ops::*` verb gets them from `batch::get_dag_tips`, and that **owns the drain** ⇒ D2-forbidden here ⇒ **there was NO WAY to construct a sendable event over the resident socket at all.** Resolved by **`TipTracker`**, a resident-held DAG frontier updated from live ingest **and** our own sends — *`get_dag_tips` exists because a short-lived control connection has no memory; a resident has one.* Anchors invalidated at **every session start** (a reconnect is exactly when a frontier silently goes stale — automatic, not remembered by a caller) and re-seeded **once per session per Space** over its **own short-lived control connection** (D2-legal, never per message). *Honest limit: across a gap the tips are stale — not corruption, it yields an event that reads as concurrent, which resolution already settles.* **⚠️ THE FINDING THAT NEARLY ENTERED THE RECORD AS A DEFECT:** `ingest.received` sat at **0** through every Chat send, including from a separate CLI connection — the obvious read was *“the seam is dead”*. **FALSE:** `xgen-node/src/fanout.rs:301` is `if rid == author_id` (skip), and the node's own test is named `message_fans_out_to_other_members_and_excludes_author`. **Zero was CORRECT** — the resident authored every message and was never an eligible recipient; a second registered member fed it. *The answer came from the node's source, not from a plausible story.* **VERIFICATION — ATTRIBUTED PER SEAT (load-bearing):** **V1** accepted round-trip = **two-seat** (Chat, then Clair on her own inputs) · **V2** frontier anchoring (`tip_count=1` **= msg1's event_id**) = **CHAT, SINGLE-SEAT** · **V3** live ingest = **two-seat, independently re-driven** (Chat *“hello from Bob”* @3; Clair *“sanity from bob”* @0→1 then sustained to **44**, `dropped:0`) · **V4/V5/V6 = Clair**. **🔑 V2 IS DELIBERATELY NOT TWO-SEAT:** Clair's V6 first-sends all re-anchored and all came back `accepted`, which re-confirms the re-anchor **path fires**, **NOT** that the anchor was **the frontier** — a root-anchored event is equally valid and equally accepted. ***AN `accepted` OUTCOME PROVES THE ANCHOR WAS VALID, NEVER OPTIMAL — CHAINING IS OBSERVABLE ONLY IN THE TIPS.*** Standing rule, so nobody reads a wall of green accepts as proof the frontier is tracked. *(Clair raised it herself rather than let the tick blur.)* **CLAIR'S V4 METHOD, accepted as better than the obvious one:** loopback acks in ~1 ms so kill-during-burst almost never catches a send in `pending` — **she refused to approximate**; `NtSuspendProcess` (**self-tested on a throwaway counter BEFORE being depended on**) freezes the node, whose kernel socket keeps accepting small writes ⇒ sends land in `pending` with no ack possible. **Arm A (positive control):** suspend→fire 2→`{null,null,READY}`→resume→both `accepted`, window **1854 ms** (inside the sweep ⇒ real acks). **Arm B:** suspend→fire 3→all outstanding→**kill from frozen**→all three `failed`, reason **“connection ended before the node confirmed”** (the **teardown drain**, not the write-fail arm), none `accepted`/`timed_out`, first-send→kill **1542 ms ≪ 10 000** so the sweep is excluded; **not-wedged proven by a LIVE `attempt` climbing 1→2→3**, not a status read that could succeed against a stalled drain. ***An arm that proves the window is real before the other arm claims a failure is the leg J-501 was missing.*** **CLAIR'S V5 DISAMBIGUATION:** a 40-send burst showed an apparent inbound plateau and she **did not accept it at face value** — the burst's own acks flowed (0→21→40) while writes were outstanding (*a drain blocked on writes cannot read acks*) and were themselves delayed ⇒ the bottleneck was the **node**, not the drain (cause: a burst on an un-anchored Space fires up to 40 concurrent re-anchor connections). Phase-0 amended in place. **⚠️ LEG-C CONSEQUENCE: a frozen peer produces NO lifecycle transition at all, so the gap item will not appear — do not describe the indicator as detecting “the node is down”; it detects a BROKEN SOCKET.** ② **Latent: the re-anchor herd** (N concurrent first-sends → N control connections; no leak per V6; Leg D's composer sends one at a time — filed, not fixed). ③ Chat's gate hygiene: `cargo test` returned **`0/0/0`** twice (N-117 exe lock from a leftover `cargo-tauri`; then a real compile error) — *a gate that did not execute is not a gate that passed*, both discarded. **No new D, no new `core`.** **OWED unchanged:** ConnStats row-swap + all Leg C/D appearance → Ms Design. **🟢 NEXT-ACTIVE = M-RP6.3 Leg C — R5 stream wrap + the LIVE MUTABLE ITEM CLASS** (reads the `$common` `ingest` store + the Leg-A status surface; **the terminal item must NOT be drawn as permanent** — any `focus` resumes a parked resident). **Entry (Rule 0): CLAUDE.md PLAY → JOURNAL J-546 → `tasks/M_RP6_3_COMPOSER.md` §8.** → JOURNAL J-546 · `tasks/M_RP6_3_COMPOSER.md` v1.2 · ROADMAP v5.13.

activate the tab first, or a zero reads as a catastrophic fill failure that never happened. **DEVIATIONS (Rule 6), all four accepted:** ① `tick`→**`tickNow`** — Chat's suggested name **collided with Svelte's `tick`**, already imported in the same file for the scroll machine (her catch; > ### ✅ **THE FILED WIRE FIX LANDS — `IngestEvent.event_type` → `type` — CLOSED (J-551). A Chat micro-change ahead of Leg D Phase-0, taken as the NAMED exception to the code-is-Clair's seat rule and recorded so the exception is visible rather than silent. Three files, code + comments only, zero `.rs`, zero `core`.** **🔑 IT WAS THREE READERS, NOT ONE — AND THE "HARMLESS" FALLBACK STOPPED TYPE-CHECKING.** The brief said `wireType()` reads `type` first with an `event_type` fallback *"so nothing breaks"*. `grep -r "event_type" ui/` returned **3 files / 7 hits**, and reader two was `.type ?? e.event_type` — after the rename that property **does not exist on `IngestEvent`**. Re-cast once: `const w = e as { type?: string; event_type?: string }; return w.type ?? w.event_type;` *A fix scoped from a description rather than from the code would have shipped broken.* **⚠️ → N-138 — THE FINDING THAT OUTLIVES THE FIX: NO `ui/**` PACKAGE RUNS A TYPECHECK.** `client`/`node` = `dev`/`build`/`preview`; `sampler` adds `test`. **No `svelte-check`, no `tsc`, anywhere.** So **`npm test` 114 and `vite build` 192/169 CANNOT FAIL ON A TYPE ERROR** — they are honest about runtime + module graph and silent about exactly the defect class a rename produces. **Never cite the frontend floors as evidence a typed change is correct** (the N-108 error in a new costume); the DIRECT evidence is the grep + the read of every reader, floors only corroborate. → **M-RP-TYPECHECK — admit a typecheck to the frontend gate set**, FILED, unscoped, Joe's; deliberately NOT smuggled in, since first run will surface a backlog and mid-leg is the wrong moment to find it. **RUNTIME UNCHANGED BY CONSTRUCTION:** types erase, and the one executable line is semantically identical either way — so **no live re-drive was performed and none is claimed**; gates were still run, because *"cannot break" and "did not break" are two statements and only one is free.* **`wireType()` + fallback KEPT** (one thing at a time): its test is part of the **114** floor, so deleting it would move a gate inside a fix meant to move nothing — retitled from *"if a future interface fix renames it"* (a future that has now happened) to a **legacy** read. Two comments describing the drift as live were rewritten: **a comment asserting a fixed defect is a false record** (D-065). **The declaration now carries a DO-NOT-REVERT note** — `wire.rs:476` is `#[serde(rename = "type")] pub event_type: EventType`, the Rust name exists **only because `type` is a Rust keyword**; frontend-only by construction. **⚠️ TRAP NO GREP CAN REACH, CARRIED INTO LEG D:** synthetic `ingest.push` payloads live in **CDP eval strings**, not the repo — every future drive must push `type`, and *a drive that passes only through the fallback is testing the wrong path.* **FLOORS HELD EXACTLY: npm 114 · vite 192 client / 169 sampler.** `cargo test` not re-run and said so. **ROADMAP deliberately untouched** — no milestone changed state. **🟢 NEXT-ACTIVE unchanged = M-RP6.3 Leg D — R6 composer + send path**, now at Phase-0 grounding (own-sends-render-nowhere / C-4 pressure · what R6 wraps · what of the four-way `SendOutcome` crosses the Tauri boundary). → JOURNAL J-551 · `ui/docs/xgen-ui-notes.md` v1.0 (N-138) · `tasks/M_RP6_3_COMPOSER.md` v1.7.

> ### 🟢 **M-RP6.3 Leg D — R6 COMPOSER + SEND PATH — PHASE-0 LOCKED (J-552). Design + records only, NO CODE. `tasks/M_RP6_3_COMPOSER.md` v1.8 §9.11 (D-1…D-5 + twelve user-facing locks + C-4 amended in place). Gates untouched.** **⚠️ THE FIRST PHASE-0 ON THIS ARC THAT DID NOT COME BACK SHELL-ONLY — LEG D TOUCHES `.rs` AND `core`, AND A MILESTONE IS INSERTED AHEAD OF IT.** **TWO BRIEF CORRECTIONS, BOTH MATERIAL:** the four-way is `accepted`·**`rejected`**·`timed_out`·`failed` (`resident.rs:706`) — *in-flight is NOT an outcome, it is the state before one exists*; `rejected` is a deterministic refusal with a wire `code`, **a message that will never arrive**. And **`event_id` arrives WITH the outcome** — `send_message` resolves once — so the brief's *"pending store keyed by `event_id`"* **is not constructible**; keyed by a **client-minted local id**, real id stitched on at outcome. **🔑 C-4 AMENDED IN PLACE (§9.11.2):** the node excludes the author from fan-out **BY IDENTITY, not conn_id** (`fanout.rs:305`, EV-D2), so a send returns `accepted` and **the stream does not change** — the user's own words render nowhere. **The brief's *"mirror with a defined death — dropped when the real event arrives"* CANNOT WORK: the real event NEVER arrives.** LOCKED — C-4 governs **inbound** unchanged; **outbound is a NAMED exception**: a session-mortal, never-federated echo store in `$common`. *The awkwardness becomes a DOCUMENTED property rather than a leak.* **TWELVE USER-FACING LOCKS**, incl. echo in a **`$common` store** (the C-10 argument, stronger: *a lost outage row is an omission; a lost sentence you just typed is the app eating your words*) · **THREE visual states, not two** — sent · **unresolved** · not sent (*collapsing `timed_out` either way is the D6 lie verbatim*) · **retry by status**: `failed` free, `rejected` never, `timed_out` **only behind a double-post warning** · the **C-6 head marker must cover the user's OWN sends** (*they never read the messages they lost; they wrote the one they lost*) · self **not** rendered as its own hash tail · auto-scroll on own send · **N windows**, not "two" · no room latched ⇒ **type yes, send no**. **⚠️ THE SILENT QUEUE D6 FORBIDS IS ALREADY SHIPPED, AND ONLY A COMPOSER MAKES IT REACHABLE:** `send_message` never checks link state (`desktop.rs:331`) and `io.outbound_rx.recv()` is polled **ONLY inside `run_session`'s `select!`** (`resident.rs:418`) — between sessions **nothing drains the queue**, so **the promise never resolves**, the message sits, and on reconnect the drain writes it **minutes later, unannounced**. **`SEND_ACK_TIMEOUT` (10 s, swept at 1 s) ages only sends already WRITTEN — a queued-but-never-written send has NO TIMER AT ALL.** *Nobody was careless: Leg B is scrupulous everywhere it writes (`"Never written → say so"`) — this is the seam between two correct pieces.* **LOCKED (D1): bound the wait; on expiry return `failed`, NOT `timed_out`** — *`failed` = never reached the wire, exactly true; `timed_out` would claim the node may hold it, the D6 lie inverted.* The frontend lifecycle guard is **the nicety**; **the timeout is the guarantee** (§0 G-4). *Rejected: failing the whole queue on teardown — it destroys a message the user may still want offered under D6's "send when reconnected".* **CONSEQUENCE: C2's zero-Rust shape DOES NOT REPEAT; `cargo test` moves off 1541 by design.** **🔑 A USER QUESTION ABOUT EMOJI RETIRED CHAT'S OWN RECOMMENDATION — THE BEST MOMENT IN THE PASS.** `details` is live (`message.svelte:71`, W-13 drop-unknown, `detailsCount` = render truth) ⇒ a status widget needs **zero `core`** to mount. **BUT grouped continuation rows suppress the WHOLE header line — name AND `details`** (`message.svelte:149`) ⇒ three sends in a row and **a `failed` third message looks exactly like the delivered first.** D6's mirror broken by a `core` render rule, in the commonest send pattern there is — *locks #6 and #9 were in direct tension and neither Chat nor the brief had noticed.* Chat recommended **perforating grouping**. Joe then asked *"what if I want to add an emoji to one of the grouped messages?"* — **and grounding THAT answered the send-status question instead:** `bodyExtras` sits **BELOW the body, OUTSIDE the `{#if !grouped}` block — grouping-immune by position** — and `types.ts:69` has named it for *attachments / reactions* all along. **So a reaction on a continuation row was never a problem, and Chat's recommendation was WRONG.** Suppression is **CORRECT**: repeating name+time on every row of a run is exactly what grouping exists to remove. ***Punching a hole in a right rule to admit one tenant is the wrong repair — the tenant was in the wrong socket.*** **LOCKED: send-status → `bodyExtras`, and the socket gets BUILT** (declared but never rendered, `message.svelte:29` ⇒ a `core` change — but **building a socket that was always designed**, not perforating a rule that was always right). **`types.ts:67–69` corrected with it** — *send-status led* leaves the `details` list, because that line was written **before a composer existed, when nobody had asked whether send-status is HEADER CHROME or PER-ROW STATE.** It is per-row state — the same category as a reaction, not the same category as an author name. **⚠️ NO PROVISIONAL-TIMESTAMP FIELD, and the reason is not cost:** **`SendOutcome` carries NO timestamp**, so even on `accepted` there is nothing to correct the time TO — *a provisional marker that can never resolve is worse than none*. **FILED:** own rows keep a client-minted time for the session and **may order differently for the author than for everyone else in the room**. **🔑 JOE INSERTS A MILESTONE, AND THE ARGUMENT IS HIS:** build `bodyExtras` **fixture-driven FIRST** — the R5 precedent exactly (M-RP5.6 built the stream on fixtures; Leg C wrapped it live four milestones later). ***A socket designed against a single static led will hold a single static led.*** Reactions are the far richer consumer — N mounts/row, runtime add/remove, interactive, on continuation rows — so **design against the strong consumer and send-status is trivially a second tenant**. **What the build teaches that a walk cannot: `cid()` under N MOUNTS × M ROWS.** Registry **134 quiescent** underwrites every verify leg on this arc; three mounts on twenty rows is **sixty new ids or sixty collisions, and which is NOT KNOWN**. *Answer it on a fixture bench, not during a composer drive where it would look like the composer's fault.* → **M-RP6.9**. **⚠️ THE FENCE ON M-RP6.9, BINDING:** the container renders `WidgetMount[]` and **never learns what a tag or reaction IS** — sampler passes fixture mounts, a later arc passes real ones, **and the component does not change between those two events**; *that is what makes it a container to complete rather than a stub to replace.* **Fixtures SAMPLER-ONLY** — in the real client it renders **NOTHING** until fed (*a client rendering invented tags is fake data on screen*, N-091/D-065) · assets **locally bundled, never remote URLs** (**D-111 must not be pre-figured even by a fixture**) · **NO `ReactionDescriptor`, no wire shape, no protocol, no attribution** — *a data shape invented before the protocol exists is a shape the protocol then has to satisfy*, and **who reacted is IDENTITY DATA, on the no-anonymity core: Joe's.** **⚠️ #11b MULTI-DEVICE — NAMED, NOT SOLVED, NOT LEG D'S:** exclusion by identity while fan-out reaches **every connection** of each recipient ⇒ other people's messages reach all of their devices **and yours reach none of yours**; type on the laptop and **the sentence does not exist on the phone** — not *until reload*, **at all**. *Joe: "this awaits us from day one, and I keep it in mind the whole time."* **The encouraging half: the node DID persist the event — fan-out is live delivery, NOT the record** ⇒ **the hole closes at M-RP6.4**, under a constraint written now while free: **backfill reads the EVENT STORE and must return the requesting identity's OWN events** (*built by replaying fan-out, the hole would silently persist*). **LEG ORDER — 🟢 D1 send-path honesty (Rust) FIRST**, because it is a **LIVE D6 violation** depending on nothing else · **M-RP6.9 `bodyExtras` container (`core`, fixture-driven)** · **D2 composer + echo store (`$common`)** · **D3 send-status widget (`$common`)**. **⚠️ FLOORS: cargo 1541/0/62 (D1 MOVES IT BY DESIGN) · npm 114 · vite 192 client / 169 sampler · sampler 386 · client registry 134 quiescent.** **FILED: M-RP-REACTIONS** — blob-backed custom reactions (custom sets, animated, >16px, **not unicode**); **discussion DELIBERATELY DEFERRED to the milestone that builds the real interface**, no design record · no authoritative timestamp on `accepted` · the M-RP6.4 event-store constraint. **No new D, no new N.** **OWED unchanged:** ConnStats row-swap + **all Leg C/D appearance** → Ms Design — who should know **`bodyExtras` is a socket two future arcs will also mount into.** **Entry (Rule 0): CLAUDE.md PLAY → JOURNAL J-552 → `tasks/M_RP6_3_COMPOSER.md` §9.11 → §1 D1–D6.** → JOURNAL J-552 · `tasks/M_RP6_3_COMPOSER.md` v1.8 · ROADMAP v5.18.

> ### ✅ **M-RP6.3 Leg D1 — SEND-PATH HONESTY — CLOSED (J-553). Commit `53dab37`, TWO FILES, +241/−11 (`desktop.rs` + `resident.rs`), scope confirmed by `git show --stat`. Zero `ui/**`, zero `core`, zero node, zero sampler. Runbook v1.2 COMPLETED, §8 close written.** **CARGO 1546/0/62 ACROSS 56 TERMINATOR LINES — CLAIR'S HANDBACK REPRODUCED EXACTLY under an independent Chat re-drive; third consecutive two-seat leg on this arc that did.** **The defect closed:** `send_message` never checked link state and `outbound_rx.recv()` is polled **only inside `run_session`'s `select!`**, so an outage left the queue undrained — **the promise never resolved** and the drain wrote the message **minutes later, unannounced**. Now `await_send_outcome` bounds the wait and returns **`failed`, never `timed_out`**, **and the drain skips a request whose `reply` oneshot is closed and logs it** — without that half, bounding the wait would have created the MIRROR violation (*told not sent, then sent*). **🔑 THE RUNBOOK WAS WRONG AND THE IMPLEMENTER CAUGHT IT BEFORE WRITING A LINE.** v1.0 §3.1 said bound with `SEND_ACK_TIMEOUT` — but `pending` is stamped at **write** time and the sweep resolves at *write* + timeout + one tick, while the caller's wait starts **strictly earlier than the write**, so the outer bound expires first **always, for every δ > 0**: `timed_out` **unreachable by construction**, and a send that DID reach the wire reported as *never reached the wire*. **Third runbook on this arc caught by the implementer reading it whole first (J-499, J-548, this) and the SECOND that was Chat's.** *Class is stable: internally consistent section by section, contradictory ACROSS sections — and the warning about exactly that shape sat three paragraphs above the instruction that violated it.* **FIX: DERIVE, DON'T CHOOSE.** Clair proposed a named constant at 13 s; **Joe locked it DERIVED** — `PENDING_SWEEP_INTERVAL` names the bare literal (now **gone from the sweep**, not shadowed) and `SEND_QUEUE_TIMEOUT = SEND_ACK_TIMEOUT + PENDING_SWEEP_INTERVAL + 5` = **16 s**. *A hand-picked 13 goes silently wrong the day someone tunes the ack timeout up; and **no constant is provably correct** — δ is bounded by nothing (channel holds 64) — **only the DIRECTION of the error is decidable**: too large costs seconds, **too small relabels an ambiguous send as a definite failure**.* **🔑 U4 MUTATION-TESTED, NOT ASSUMED:** setting `SEND_QUEUE_TIMEOUT = SEND_ACK_TIMEOUT` — **the v1.0 instruction** — **fails it**; reverted, green. ***A test that has never failed is not yet known to be able to.*** **⚠️ V-L MEASURED 19 155 ms, NOT ~16 s — DECOMPOSED, NOT ROUNDED:** 3.143 s failed re-anchor + **16.013 s bounded wait ⇒ the bound is accurate to 13 ms**; control corroborates (1142 ms containing a 1.13 s *successful* re-anchor). → **outage latency is `re-anchor + SEND_QUEUE_TIMEOUT`**, filed to COMPOSER §9.11.8 and carried into Leg D2. *A number that disagrees with the runbook and is then EXPLAINED is worth more than one that agrees.* **§3.2 PROVEN END-TO-END AGAINST A POSITIVE CONTROL:** drop logged, then the node's **persisted store** — control present (1), abandoned absent (0). **The same grep returned 1 for the control, which is what makes the 0 mean anything**; and absence was proven from the store rather than `bob`'s client, since a second identity would have proven **fan-out**, not **non-writing**. **⚠️ → N-139 — A FALSE-NEGATIVE PROBE READS EXACTLY LIKE A GENUINE ABSENCE:** `window.__TAURI__` → `"undefined"` (no `withGlobalTauri`), `__TAURI_INTERNALS__.invoke` → `"function"`. **The v1.0 probe would have recorded a defensible-looking "V-L NOT RUN" on a working path**, skipping the leg's most important verification **with a paper trail explaining why that was correct** — J-548 / N-110 family. **Rule: a probe that gates a verification must itself be POSITIVELY CONTROLLED.** **⚠️ CHAT'S FIRST GATE RUN WAS INCONCLUSIVE, NOT A PASS — N-117 LIVE:** exit 101, **zero terminator lines, `0/0/0`**, exe held. *`0/0/0` reads exactly like a clean run to anything parsing totals.* **The holder was NOT Clair's leftovers** — both processes started **~3 h AFTER** her drive. **Rule 6 deviations, all accepted:** §3.2's predicate test is a **PROXY, named as one** (N-092a) so **V-L step 5 is that half's only end-to-end evidence** · **U1 widened** to loop `Accepted`/`TimedOut`/`Rejected` because **`Rejected` carries a `code` the others do not** and *"verbatim" is a claim about all four fields*. **THREE stale comments corrected, not one** — `resident.rs:691`'s *"always resolved exactly once"* is made **NEWLY false** by §3.2, which is exactly when a comment must travel in the same commit as the code. **🟢 NEXT-ACTIVE = M-RP6.9 — `bodyExtras` container (`core`, fixture-driven)**, then **D2 composer + echo store**, then **D3 send-status widget**. **NEW FLOORS: cargo 1546/0/62 / 56 terminators · npm 114 · vite 192 client / 169 sampler · sampler 386 (NOT re-measured — grounded BY SCOPE, zero `ui/**` in the diff, J-497) · registry 134 quiescent.** **No new D, no new `core`.** → JOURNAL J-553 · runbook v1.2 COMPLETED · `tasks/M_RP6_3_COMPOSER.md` v1.9 · `ui/docs/xgen-ui-notes.md` v1.1 (N-139) · ROADMAP v5.19.

> ### 🟢 **M-RP6.9 — `bodyExtras`: THE PER-ROW MESSAGE CONTAINER (`core`, fixture-driven) — PHASE-0 LOCKED (J-554). Design + records only, NO CODE. Deliverable: `tasks/M_RP6_9_BODY_EXTRAS.md` v1.0 ACTIVE. Gates untouched, not re-measured, and says so.** **⚠️ THE QUESTION THE MILESTONE WAS INSERTED TO ANSWER IS ANSWERED BY READING — AND THE FRAMING WAS A FALSE DICHOTOMY.** J-552: *"sixty new registered ids — or sixty COLLISIONS, and WHICH ONE IS NOT KNOWN."* **Neither. As HEAD stands it is sixty ZEROS.** `cid()` (`message.svelte:79`) is called with **hardcoded literals only** (`'avatar'`/`'name'`/`'body'`), and mounts render `<W {...d.props} />` (`:167`) — **no `id` reaches a mount**; `fixture-widget.svelte` says so in its own header (*"deliberately does NOT register … keeps the registry delta predictable"*). **The socket has never been asked to mint an id, so it has never had the opportunity to collide.** ⇒ **no `core` defect, none brought to Joe as one.** **🔑 THE ANSWER IS TRUE ONLY UNTIL THE REAL TENANT ARRIVES — WHICH IS WHY THE MILESTONE STILL EARNS ITS PLACE:** a reaction is interactive **and will register**, and with no container-supplied id `envelope.ts:39` falls back to `` `${typeClass}#${++ordinal}` `` — **a module-level counter, unstable across remounts, against which no baseline can be taken** (N-132's whole premise). *Joe's argument survives its own answer.* **⚠️ THE FINDING THAT OUTLIVES THE MILESTONE — A REGISTRY COLLISION SHRINKS THE COUNT AND SAYS NOTHING:** `debug.ts` `registry` is a `Map`, `register()` is `.set()` (**duplicate id silently overwrites, no warning**) and `unregister()` is `.delete(id)` on destroy — **so the loser's unmount deletes the SURVIVOR's entry too**. A collision presents as the registry **going DOWN**, indistinguishable from a clean unmount, **squarely in N-132's blind spot**. *Every verify leg on this arc keys off a quiescent count and the failure mode of that instrument had never been characterised.* **V-7 MEASURES IT** — two mounts given one key, count watched to FALL, reverted: *a failure mode only reasoned about is not yet known to occur.* **🔑 THE DEFECT THAT IS REAL IS IN THE KEY:** `` `${m.widgetId}-${i}` `` — **index-composed, in BOTH sockets** (`message.svelte:73`, `message-stream.svelte:111`). Static lists never bit it; under runtime add/remove, removing mount 0 of `[A,B,C]` re-keys `B-1`→`B-0` and **Svelte destroys+recreates every mount after the removal point** — state loss + animation restart on an *unrelated* removal. *The "paid twice" cost Joe predicted, found before either tenant paid it.* Quieter second: resolution `$derived` over `widgets[m.widgetId]` re-derives on the **reference**, so **in-place mutation of the registry object does nothing** — **the D-119 `$state` `Set` lesson in a second costume.** **FIVE LOCKS TAKEN, NOT ASKED** (Joe restated the grant mid-session: *"mine: appearance + architectural decisions; yours: anything else"*) — all container **mechanics**, zero protocol/store/federation/identity: **D-1 the container supplies a stable `id`, the widget may ignore it** (`<W id={…} {...d.props} />`, props spread LAST; *passing `id` is not learning what the contents MEAN — `id` is this codebase's universal envelope contract*), applied to **both** sockets and **measurably inert on `details` today — which is a VERIFICATION LEG (V-2), not an assumption** · **D-2 `WidgetMount.mountKey?: string`**, optional, falling back **byte-identically to today's key** — one field fixes keying churn **and** id stability for both sockets **without a `ReactionDescriptor` or any wire shape** · **D-3 `resolveMounts()` extracted to a pure tested `core` module** (`mounts.ts`) — the logic is **written twice with ZERO tests** (`core`'s `resolve.ts`/`resolve.test.ts` are **layout**, unrelated), so **`npm test` moves for an honest reason rather than an invented one** · **D-4 a tombstone drops `bodyExtras`, matching `details`** — the container **cannot** tell an attachment (must vanish) from a reaction (arguably survives), *the fence forbids it knowing*, so one conservative rule, `bodyExtrasCount` forced to 0 (J-479 precedent), **FILED reversible at M-RP-REACTIONS** · **D-5 `types.ts:67–69` corrected HERE, not at D3** — the *send-status led* line is wrong **now** and **the socket's doc-comment travels with the socket's build.** **⚠️ A FLOOR PREDICTION CHANGED BETWEEN THE WALK AND THE RUNBOOK AND SAYS SO: catalogue ≈412 → 419**, because the fixture set gained a **second, deliberately REGISTERING stub** (`fixture-reg-widget.svelte`) — the instrument that turns the read answer into a measured one (**V-6: 4 rows × 1 registering mount ⇒ exactly 4 new ids**, all `…__m-<msgid>__x-<key>`). *A floor that changes between two documents must say it changed, or the next reader cannot tell a revision from an error.* **Both numbers are ARITHMETIC, not measurement (N-108) — a difference at drive time is EXPLAINED before it is adjusted.** **⚠️ THE CLIENT VITE FLOOR MOVES ON A SAMPLER-ONLY MILESTONE, PREDICTED ON PURPOSE: 192 → 193** — the client renders **nothing** here but still **compiles** `mounts.ts`; *an unpredicted client move on a sampler-only milestone reads exactly like scope leak.* **Sampler 169 → 171. ⚠️ cargo 1546/0/62 / 56 terminators MUST NOT MOVE — zero `.rs`; any movement stops the leg. Client registry 134 quiescent** (fixtures are sampler-only; **in the real client the container renders NOTHING until fed** — N-091/D-065). **🔑 THE VERIFICATION THE MILESTONE EXISTS FOR IS V-4, ONE LINE: `bodyExtras` renders ON A GROUPED CONTINUATION ROW** — that position (below the body, **outside** `{#if !grouped}`) is the entire reason J-552 chose this socket over perforating grouping. **If V-4 fails, `bodyExtras` was the wrong socket and Joe hears it before anything else ships.** **V-5 drop-unknown is POSITIVELY CONTROLLED (N-139)** — the same probe must return the two KNOWN mounts *or the absence of the third means nothing.* **FENCE HELD, one clause narrowed honestly:** *assets locally bundled, never remote URLs* (D-111) is carried **as an inheritance note, not a live constraint** — **this milestone ships no assets at all**; *a constraint that binds nothing should say it binds nothing.* **M-RP-REACTIONS NOT reopened** (Joe's deferral), named only to fix the container's purpose. **TO MS DESIGN:** the strip renders **where there is no header line above it**, and has **TWO future tenants** (D3 send-status — sent · unresolved · not sent — and reactions: N/row, animated, >16px, runtime add/remove); *design for the second and the first is free.* Structural class `.msg-body-extras`; `.fixture-widget` styling is a stub, **not a proposal**. **No new D, no new N, no code, no gate run.** **🟢 NEXT-ACTIVE = M-RP6.9 implementation from the locked runbook (Clair)**, then **D2 composer + echo store**, then **D3 send-status widget**. → JOURNAL J-554 · `tasks/M_RP6_9_BODY_EXTRAS.md` v1.0 · ROADMAP v5.20.

Measured at `skin.css:2836`: `.message { padding: var(--sp-1) var(--sp-2) }` = **4px 8px** and `.message[data-grouped] { padding-top: 0 }` ⇒ non-grouped rows sit **8px** apart (**not 16**), and a **grouped continuation sits 4px** because it zeroes its own top padding. **⇒ the real ratio on a continuation row is 4px vs 4px — 1:1, NO belonging signal at all, on precisely the row type this milestone exists for.** *Her conclusion was right; the alternative is clipping user content, so the promise is *"the 4th tag does not move the row"*, **not** *"nothing ever moves"* · **`justify-content: flex-end` on own rows, NOT `row-reverse`** — reversing would reverse **the order of a user's reactions**, which is a reordering, not a mirror. **⚠️ §7.3 — WHAT IT DOES NOT SHIP:** her send-status glyph design is **Leg D3 intent ONLY**; composing at the bottom works free. > ### 🔑 **THE TEXT PROCESSOR IS WIRED TO NOTHING — FOUND BY A QUESTION OF JOE'S AT J-560, NOT BY ANY GATE.** He asked whether the composer used a new component or the already-authored one with the processor. **It reuses `$core/…/textarea.svelte`** (which is why N-149's 7th module appeared) — **but the processor is NOT wired to it:** `composer-panel.svelte` has **zero** mentions of `processor`/`substitutions`; `textarea.svelte` is **processor-READY by design** (*"ready, not containing"*, D-065, forwarding `{...rest}` at `:73`); **`app_client.svelte:537` ALREADY LOADS the user's rules** via `invoke('get_substitutions')`; and `substitutions-editor.svelte` is mounted **only in the sampler**. ***So the client loads your substitution rules, has no screen to edit them, and no input that applies them — a subsystem complete at both ends and connected in the middle to nothing.*** **⚠️ CHAT'S FIRST FRAMING WAS WRONG AND JOE CORRECTED IT:** Chat split "the substitutions feature" from "the text processor" — but **`clamp.ts` is literally *text-processor KIND 3*** and **D-099 defines the processor as ONE architecture: two engines × four rule-kinds**, so substitutions is **kind 1 of four**, not another layer. **Chat's safety objection collapsed on grounding too:** it argued against plugin status because disabling would kill the numeric guard — **but `plugin-list.svelte:91` greys the toggle for built-ins (`disabled: !isCustom`, "Built-in — cannot be disabled", W-13), so a `system` row has NO disable button and the hazard does not exist.** → **DIRECTION: ONE row, `Text Processing`, `kind:'system'` · `surface:'none'` · NO `component` · `settingsComponent: SubstitutionsEditor`** — the **first row that is purely a settings surface** (GridPlate minus the component). **🔒 AND IT RESOLVES J-513 BY EVIDENCE** — the **SECOND `settingsComponent`**, and the very component cited as the precedent for component-per-plugin over the declarative `settings_schema`: *one instance is a special case, two is a pattern.* **So the processor is not a tail-end item — it is what UNBLOCKS M-RP-SETTINGS**, and doing it afterwards would mean doing that milestone twice. **🔒 THE WIRING POLICY, JOE-LOCKED BEFORE THE FIELDS THAT NEED IT EXIST: default OFF everywhere; plug in only where it matters.** Enforced at **TWO** gates: **① the component must FORWARD `{...rest}`** — only `converter-field`, `number`, `textarea` do; **`password-field` and `textfield` do NOT, so processing them is structurally impossible** — and **② the consumer opts in per call site.** *An XGID, a token or a password silently rewritten by a user's own rule would be a real harm, and the design refuses it at the component level rather than trusting every future call site to remember.* **MEASURED: the ENTIRE client+common tree has TWO text-input call sites** — `composer-panel:94` (**plug in**) and `substitutions-editor:98` (**NEVER — processing the textarea where you author substitution rules is a feedback loop: typing `:)` into the rule list rewrites the rule you are writing**). **Joe's criterion, refined and accepted: COMPOSING vs CONFIGURING** — *composing is prose written in flow where the transformation is visible and correctable as it happens; configuring is a value stored and reused, where the rewrite lands silently and nobody is watching* — plus a **third, stronger category: values that must round-trip BYTE-EXACT**, a correctness rule rather than a preference. **⚠️ OPEN BEFORE IT SCOPES: does `get_substitutions` have a WRITE path on the Rust side?** Without one an in-client editor renders and applies but **cannot persist**, and the user's edits die at reload — the difference between one milestone and two.

M-RP-SETTINGS IS CLOSED** - `ROADMAP.md` L931: Leg A ✅ J-535 · Leg B ✅ J-537 · **Leg C CLOSED J-540 → the SETTINGS arc is CLOSED**, D-B → **D-120 minted** (`DECISIONS.md:4452`). **J-513 was decided at J-534 and minted at J-540; it was NEVER waiting on a second `settingsComponent`.** The `Text Processing` row is a **second TENANT of a closed mechanism** - corroboration for D-120 at best, and **D-120 does not need it**. ***The prior seat did not misread the ROADMAP; it never read it*** - the gate was carried from an older memory of the arc, asserted as current, written into J-560 + this PLAY block + `ROADMAP.md` L804 + the kickoff, **and used to recommend that Joe reorder his round.** **THE SEVENTH SELF-CONTRADICTION ON THIS ARC, ALL SEVEN CHAT'S, AND THE FIRST TO CHANGE A DECISION** - the previous six cost rework; this one cost a choice. **🔑 THE LESSON IS NOT "CHECK THE ROADMAP": a remembered gate is not a gate, and a recommendation about SEQUENCING is the highest-leverage place an ungrounded premise can sit, because it is acted on before anything downstream can catch it.** **✅ EVERYTHING ELSE IN THE BLOCK ABOVE RE-VERIFIED BY GREP AND HOLDS** (composer zero mentions · `textarea` processor-READY · `app_client.svelte:537` loads · editor sampler-only · the two call sites · the two gates · COMPOSING vs CONFIGURING). **🔑 AND THE OPEN QUESTION IS ANSWERED - THE WRITE PATH EXISTS AND IS TESTED** (`desktop.rs:421` `set_substitutions`, registered `:904` → `app.rs:271` `write_substitutions_section`, round-trip / error / clearing tests) - **but that was never the problem. ⚠️ D-101 IS LIVE IN THE SHIPPING CLIENT:** `desktop.rs:677` calls `app::clean_slate_config()` at launch and `app.rs:414` **deletes `xgen-client_config.toml` and regenerates from seed.** ***So the write works and the file is deleted at the next launch. A settings screen for user-authored data, in front of a config file wiped at every launch, is the sharpest available instance of what D6 and D-065 exist to prevent - and it would have shipped looking finished.***

> ### 🔒 **THE NEXT FIVE, SEQUENCED (Joe-locked, J-564). The ordering argument is about WHAT MAKES LATER WORK SMALLER, not about size.** **① 🟢 `M-RP-MSG-NEWLINE` — preserve typed line breaks in the rendered message** · **② `M-RP-PROCESSOR-SEED`** · **③ `core`'s prop-less `Record<string, Component>`** · **④ `M-RP-COMPOSER-SCROLL`** · **⑤ `M-RP-PROCESSOR-RENDER`.** **🔑 WHY ① IS FIRST, AND IT IS NOT SIZE: solving line breaks in CSS TAKES `<br>` OFF KIND-4's ALLOWLIST** — *every element removed from a sanitiser's allowlist is one less thing that has to be right forever.* **🔑 WHY ③ PRECEDES ⑤: two of its six sites are `message.svelte` and `message-stream.svelte`, the exact files ⑤ will open** — fix the types first and the message family is touched **once with correct types instead of twice.** **⑤ IS LAST DELIBERATELY** — by then `<br>` is handled, the family is typed and the scroll is right, **so it can be ONLY about the sanitiser.** *Chat named ①-vs-② as genuinely interchangeable; the only orderings carrying a real argument are newline-before-RENDER and typing-before-RENDER.*

> ### ✅ **M-RP-MSG-NEWLINE — PRESERVE TYPED LINE BREAKS IN THE RENDERED MESSAGE. [SUPERSEDED AT J-565 — CLOSED; read the ✅ close block below, not this filing. Its `🟢 NEXT` is spent and its ② COST line — *"deciding whether the tombstone and `(edited)` marker inherit it"* — rests on a premise grounding disproved: they are SIBLINGS, not descendants.]** **Joe's report: Shift+Enter works in the composer but the break never reaches the message.** ⚠️ **THE NEWLINE IS NOT LOST — IT IS COLLAPSED AT PAINT.** `composer-panel.svelte:67` is `draft.trim()`, which strips leading/trailing whitespace only and **does not touch interior newlines**, so the `\n` is in the sent text; `message.svelte` renders `body` as a **plain text node** and a `white-space` sweep of `skin.css` returns only `nowrap` rules, **none on the message body** — HTML's default collapses the newline to a space. ***The data is fine; the renderer throws it away. What the user sent is not what the user is shown — the D6 / D-065 class, quiet rather than loud.*** **① USER-VISIBLE: a deliberate paragraph break silently becomes a space, in every message, with no way for the user to tell whether it SENT that way or RENDERED that way. ② COST: likely one declaration (`white-space: pre-wrap`) plus deciding whether the tombstone and `(edited)` marker inherit it.** **⚠️ LEG 1 IS A LIVE PROBE, NOT THE FIX** — the diagnosis is **two greps, not a measurement**: read `\n` out of the echo store's `text`, and **if it is absent there the cause flips to a data bug and the fix moves.** *N-153, one day old: a wrong cause that reproduces looks exactly like a fixed one.* **⚠️ SEPARATE AND NOT ASSUMED: whether `\n` survives a real WIRE round-trip is untested and needs a node** — an open leg, not a presumption. **🔑 THIS IS A CORRECTNESS DEFECT, NOT A SKIN ITEM** — the fix lives in `skin.css`, but routing it through M-RP-SKIN would park a *what-you-sent-is-not-what-you-see* bug behind a large late arc.

> ### 🟡 **THE DESIGN WALK BEHIND IT — SIX MEASURED FINDINGS AND TWO JOE CORRECTIONS (J-598).** ① **THE OUTAGE IS BIDIRECTIONAL** — a send is queued as **intent, not an event** (`resident.rs:705-727`; the drain builds/signs/anchors at write time), so during an outage **the user's own message never becomes an event at all**; no persistent outbox; `SendOutcome` stays honest. ② **THE CATCH-UP MECHANISM IS BUILT, TESTED AND NEVER CALLED BY THE GUI** — `SyncRequest`→`HistoryBatch`+`SyncComplete` (`app.rs:1752-1784`), `collect_sync_history` (`fanout.rs:478`) member-only/paginated/cursor-resumable, five passing tests, used by `ai_service.rs` + `batch.rs`; **`resident.rs` never issues one**, and no sync cursor is persisted. ③ 🔑 **THE CLIENT HAS NO MESSAGE HISTORY AT ALL** — `ingest` is in-memory, capped `INGEST_CAP = 500`, with a `dropped` counter: **a session window, not a cache**; the startup block does **no history fetch of any kind** ⇒ **every start is a cold start with an empty conversation**, and the blackout gap is a special case of a permanent one. ④ 🔒 **SYSTEM ENTRIES ARE THREAD ENTRIES LOCAL TO ONE USER (Joe, WhatsApp *delete-just-for-user*)** ⇒ **the rendered thread is a PER-USER PROJECTION of the shared record, not the shared record**; four consequences agreed — the thread becomes a **merge**, ordering meets two clocks, persistence is session-scoped **but COUPLED to message persistence** (or a reopened thread shows a gap with its explanation missing), and **additive and subtractive are ONE facility** so delete-for-me lands in the same local layer. **Drafted `D-130`; number and wording Joe's.** ⑤ 🔒 **NO MESSAGE CACHE (Joe): *"the client is just reader-sender. doesnt hold any users data."*** 🔑 **The lock sits a LEVEL ABOVE the question, so the D-093 crypto-shred tension never has to be adjudicated — the copy that would create it never exists.** ⚠️ **One measured exception: the address book DOES persist third-party data**, bounded by `T1_DEFAULT_RETENTION_DAYS = 182`; ⚠️ **whether eviction is WIRED or merely DECLARED is UNMEASURED — Chat's.** ⑥ 🔒 **THE OUTBOX SURVIVES THAT LOCK** — Joe's *"purge … not whole enblock"* maps onto the four-way `SendOutcome`: **purge on `accepted`, keep on everything else**; per-item is load-bearing because a block purge discards a `timed_out` beside a confirmed one. 🔑 **An outbox item BECOMES a cache the instant the node accepts it — which is exactly when the rule drops it.** 🔓 Open: post-reconnect anchor timestamp · `timed_out` auto-retry vs manual · the sibling's name. → J-598.

> ### 🔑 **H1 / H2 — THE ADDRESS BOOK AT REST, AND THE ONE PLACE THE THESIS ACTUALLY BITES (J-598).** ⚠️ **THERE IS NO IDENTITY LOOKUP VERB ON THE WIRE — NONE.** Records are **derived from a Space DAG drain** (`fill_and_members`, `desktop.rs:644-697`), independently confirming `M_RP_ADDRESS_BOOK.md` §5. ⇒ Joe's *send pubkeys, get visit cards* is **new protocol** (wire `xgen-core/src/wire/types.rs:45` · node handler · **and the SPEC CHAPTER — in a protocol project the spec IS the deliverable**). **H1 — drop the persistence, keep the book in memory — needs NO protocol at all** and is nearly free: `loadMembers` fires the fill **unconditionally on every Space change**, so the file only buys the pre-first-fill window and restarts. 🔒 **Joe relaxed the resource lens** (*"i dont mind if we will need to add some cli … we will test it"*) — build cost is **not** the objection. 🔑 **THE THESIS ANSWER, ASKED IN THOSE TERMS: THE VERB IS THESIS-NEUTRAL; THE SCOPE RULE IS THESIS-BEARING.** No-anonymity means *within a Space you know who you are talking to*, **NOT** *any identity is globally queryable by anyone holding a pubkey* — **an unscoped verb converts the first into the second: a thesis change made by accident, at the wire level.** Member-scoped, as `collect_sync_history` already is. ⚠️ **Recorded as Chat's READING from the project record — the Ch0–Ch2 read is OWED before any of it reaches a spec.** 🔓 H1 and H2 are Joe's and belong to neither current milestone. → J-598.

> ### ✅ **M-RP-PANEL-INERT — `entity-panel` GAINS AN INERT MODE — CLOSED (J-596). 2 files, `xgen-client`/core + sampler only (`ui/core/.../entity-panel.svelte` + `ui/sampler/src/app_sampler.svelte`); ZERO `.rs`, ZERO `skin.css`, ZERO widgets/, ZERO `entity-item`/`rooms-panel`/`spaces-panel`.** 🔑 **BORN FROM CLAIR'S GOOD STOP AT THE TOP OF LEG B.** Grounding `entity-panel` for R7 showed the Phase-0 §G2 *"literal template"* was a claim narrower than the thing it described: **`entity-panel` is an interactive single-select listbox** (`.entity-panel-option{cursor:pointer}`+focus ring; `<li>` wires `onclick=selectAt`+`onkeydown`; `selected` is `$bindable`, written from **inside** `selectAt`), **not** a neutral renderer. R1/R2 are correct only because they **close a loop R7 cannot** — `onActivate→selection.set()`(the bus)→their `$derived selected` flows back; R7 is forbidden the bus (L15) ⇒ its one-way `selected` **drifts on click and sticks**, and in a group room a click **manufactures** a highlight L16 says must be null — colliding with **three Joe locks** (L7 inert · L16 DM-only · line 284 not-a-selection-surface). **Clair surfaced it instead of shipping click-to-select on a members list.** 🔒 **JOE RULED C, NOT A/B:** Chat (and Clair) ranked "reimplement the list inside R7 (~70 lines)" first, having read Leg B §1's *"do not touch entity-panel"* as a statement about **COST**; **it is about SCOPE.** Measured: the `entity-panel` change is **~10 lines**. ***The avoidance was seven times the size of the thing avoided, would have left R7 divergent from every sibling, and would have been thrown away when C landed anyway. Neither of us would have caught it by re-reading; Joe caught it by asking whether the decision was not already made.*** **SHIPPED:** new prop `interactive?: boolean` (**default `true`** = today verbatim); `false` ⇒ `<ul>` `listbox`→`list`, `<li>` `option`→`listitem`, drops `tabindex`/`aria-selected`/`onclick`/`onkeydown`/roving; **`selected` STILL flows to `EntityItem`** — the row *renders* state, never *produces* it. 🔑 **THE ARIA CHANGE IS THE LOAD-BEARING HALF, NOT THE CURSOR** — keeping `role=listbox/option` while suppressing cursor+hover would announce a selectable listbox to a screen reader, *lying to assistive tech harder than it lies visually.* 🔑 **TWO STATIC-ROLE BRANCHES, NOT ONE DYNAMIC — svelte-check forced it:** a single `<li role={interactive?…}>` moved the floor to **0/35/16** (`a11y_no_noninteractive_tabindex`: a dynamic role can't be proven interactive, so a nonnegative `tabindex` reads as tabindex-on-a-noninteractive-element); split into `{#if interactive}` static-role branches sharing a `{#snippet}` body → **NOT a `svelte-ignore`**, floor back to **0/34/15** honestly. ⚠️ **MEASURED CORRECTION — THE SPEC §3 "BULLETED FAILURE" DOES NOT OCCUR (N-116 on the spec's own claim):** `.entity-panel{list-style:none}` is on the `<ul>` and `list-style-type` **inherits**, so the inert `<li>` inherits `none` (measured, identical to the option row); the highlight radius lives on the inner `.entity-item`, not the `<li>`. ⇒ **inert rows render correctly with the class `.entity-panel-listitem` wired and NO skin rule** — no bullet/pointer/ring; the class is a hook, a rule is optional. The one surviving affordance is `.entity-item:hover` (Joe's, the L7 carve-out, now the **only one of six left**). **CDP-VERIFIED REAL SAMPLER 9422 (Rule 5, every leg driven; sampler then stopped cleanly, Vite reaped, no leak):** catalogue **427===unique** (+8 for the one inert fixture) · inert getter `interactive:false`+`selected` present, `#spaces` `interactive:true` (default holds) · inert DOM `list`/`listitem`/no-tabindex/no-aria-selected/`firstItemSelected:true` · interactive control `#spaces` `listbox`/`option`/tabindex/aria-selected intact · **behavioural with a POSITIVE CONTROL (N-163):** synthetic click on an inert row = `selected` UNCHANGED, on the interactive `#spaces` control = CHANGED (so clicks reach handlers ⇒ inert non-response is genuine, N-099/N-139 guarded) · sweep: five defaults `interactive:true`, inert alone `false`. **⚠️ ONE DEVIATION FLAGGED (Rule 6):** `app_sampler.svelte` is not in §1's TOUCHED list (only `entity-panel`+`skin.css`); the DoD requires inert verified *in the DOM* ⇒ one inert fixture cell added to the D-097 test-bed, the five existing cells being the default-mode regression fixture. **Floor `svelte-check` 0/34/15, re-measured.** Records fold in Chat's Leg-B amendments (`M_RP_MEMBERS.md` §G2, `RUNBOOK…LEG_B.md` §0c/§1) + the spec (COMPLETED, §3 corrected, §6 added) + J-596 + ROADMAP. **▶️ NEXT: M-RP-MEMBERS Leg B**, which now passes `interactive={false}` and touches `entity-panel` not at all. No new D, no new N.

No branch, no vanishing region, no rule-with-an-exception. ⚠️ *Chat's three options were all SUBTRACTIVE; the question was never framed as "what could it add".* ⚠️ **AND CHAT'S OBJECTION TO THE MECHANISM WAS WRONG, DISSOLVED BY ONE SENTENCE:** Chat said `entity-item.selected` would be semantically overloaded because the user does not *pick* the counterpart — **Joe: *"when i go to dm someone, i pick him. otherwise the dm thread will not display itself."*** ⇒ opening a DM **IS** the selection. 📌 And `selected` is **NOT unplumbed** — `entity-panel.svelte:129/148/159` already passes the per-row boolean down; only a CALLER was missing ⇒ **ONE PROP**. 🔑 **THE TRAP BESIDE THE RULING — "SELECTED" MEANS TWO THINGS:** `entity-panel.selected` is a **LOCAL prop**; `selection.current` is the **GLOBAL bus** (*"ONE active selection across the whole layout"*) that R1/R2/R3 WRITE and **R8 inspector + `entity-context-menu` READ**. 🔒 **R7 passes `selected={counterpartId}` LOCALLY and MUST NOT call `selection.set()`** — otherwise **opening a DM silently changes what R8 displays**. *Two greps from shipping.* 🔒 **SELF SHIPS UNMARKED** — present, first, position is the mark; **M-RP-OWN-ROW-NAME** owns the *"Self"* label and is a STREAM milestone (D-067), and a two-row list must not carry two visual languages ⇒ in a DM the marked row is Bob, the other is you **by elimination**. DELEGATED. 🔒 **`Members`** locked as the descriptor name — **Joe CORRECTED Chat's stated house pattern to get there** (*"we have rooms, messages, composer. panel is redundant imho"*). ⚠️ **MEASURED, JOE RIGHT BY 8 TO 2:** every other `registry.ts` descriptor is BARE — `Spaces`·`Rooms`·`Messages`·`Composer`·`Plugin List`·`Grid Backdrop`·`Text Processing`·`Connection Stats`; only `Self Panel` (`:110`) and `Inspector Panel` (`:123`) carry the suffix, **and Joe is retiring those two**. 🔑 ***Chat sampled the TWO EXCEPTIONS and reported them as the CONVENTION*** — the defect class applied to a naming rule. 🔑 **R4: JOE ASKED FOR TUNING AND THERE IS NOTHING TO TUNE — `room-header` is a region id, a `REGION_NAMES` entry, a layout leaf and a fixture in two tests, and there is NO `room-header-panel.svelte`. IT IS AN EMPTY TILE.** ⇒ filed as **M-RP-ROOM-HEADER** `tasks/M_RP_ROOM_HEADER.md` v1.1 **PENDING, explicitly NOT scheduled**, so this milestone could not absorb it. ⚠️ **TWO FINDINGS INSIDE IT:** ① **"random" must become DERIVED** — randomised per render it changes on every room open, is useless as identity signal, reads as *something changed*, and is **untestable**; derived from the XGID it is an identicon and a **weak second identity channel** ⇒ belongs to **D-126**, must consume it not duplicate it. ② **Joe's normal-room ruling (*"the info will be about the room … set by the room's owner"*) DOES NOT REACH THE CLIENT:** `RoomState.topic` exists (`space/state.rs:117`, event-sourced) and the owner role is real (`KnownSpace.role`), but **`KnownRoom` is `room_id · name · joined`** and **`xgen-client` has ZERO `topic`** ⇒ **the DM half is a UI milestone; the room half is a state/wire change plus a write path — ARCHITECTURE.** 📌 **The UI had already written the answer and nobody connected it:** `spaces-panel.svelte:38` — *"`secondary`/`meta` ship UNFED — no faked topic"*; **the slots are empty BECAUSE the datum does not exist client-side.** ⚠️ **CHAT MANUFACTURED A DECISION AND WITHDREW IT** — R4 sequencing was put on Joe's list although the milestone has not opened (*"i dont want to expand this milestone much more"*). *The pressure came from Chat, not the work — the INVERSE of the usual failure.* **FLOORS RE-MEASURED TODAY, NOT INHERITED: svelte-check 0/34/15 · registry 149** (⚠️ total matches, **composition NOT established** — runbook §0b). **No new D. > ### ✅ **M-RP-DEVSERVER-GUARD — THE DEV LAUNCHER OWNS THE VITE IT STARTS — CLOSED (J-592). 4 files, ALL `.ps1` at repo root: `run-client.ps1` · `run-node.ps1` · `run-sampler.ps1` · `cdp-debug.ps1`. ZERO Rust · ZERO `ui/**` ⇒ NO FLOOR MOVES.** Task doc `tasks/M_RP_DEVSERVER_GUARD.md` v1.0 COMPLETED. **RAN BEFORE LEG B ON PURPOSE:** Leg B is frontend work; under this defect it would have been verified against a bundle hours older than the code under test **with every probe reporting success** — fixing after makes Leg B's evidence retroactively suspect, fixing during changes the instrument mid-measurement. 🔑 **THE HANDLE WAS NOT THE SERVER AND THE TREE IS FOUR DEEP (MEASURED):** `cmd.exe` (ours, the `$vite` handle) → `node` npm-cli.js → **a SECOND `cmd.exe /d /s /c vite`** → `node` vite.js, **the listener**. `Stop-Process $vite` killed **1 of 6**; npm survived and **RESPAWNED vite**. ⚠️ **The inherited brief said *"target the npm parent"* — the script's own handle sits one level ABOVE npm again.** 🔑 **AND THE PROBE ASKED THE WRONG QUESTION, WHICH IS WHY THE LEAK WAS INVISIBLE:** `Invoke-WebRequest localhost:5173` cannot tell *"MY server started"* from *"A server is listening"*; all three `vite.config.js` are `strictPort:true` (5173 client · 5174 node · 5175 sampler, READ not recalled), so the next run's own Vite **dies on the taken port** while the probe is **answered by the LEAK** and prints *"Vite ready"* ⇒ ***the app runs a STALE BUNDLE with no error anywhere.*** **The J-591 class exactly — a check that passes because something else supplied what was missing.** **TWO MORE, NEITHER IN THE BRIEF:** ③ cleanup was **not in a `finally`** (Ctrl-C or a closed console skipped it) · ④ **`TAURI_SKIP_DEVSERVER_CHECK="true"` disables Tauri's OWN devserver check** — the second alarm was switched off by hand. 📌 **FLAGGED, NOT CHANGED — 🔓 JOE'S.** **THE FIX — THREE GUARANTEES THAT COVER EACH OTHER:** ① **PRE-FLIGHT REFUSAL** (port held ⇒ abort NAMING the holder's PID + command line) · ② **OWNERSHIP ASSERTION** (the listener must be a **DESCENDANT of the process we spawned**; presence of a listener proves nothing) · ③ **TREE KILL IN `finally`** (`exit` inside `try` still runs it, so abort paths clean up too). 🔑 **THE RESIDUAL GAP IS NAMED, NOT HIDDEN:** a console closed mid-run skips `finally` — **① then makes the NEXT run REFUSE LOUDLY instead of lying.** *Neither half suffices alone: ① leaves the check-then-start race, ② wastes the 15 s wait.* 🔒 **JOE RULED SCOPE — ALL THREE, NOT THE CLIENT ALONE.** He asked whether node could be skipped; measurement answered: **two of three sanctioned launchers start a Vite** (`run-client_debug.lnk` → 5173, `run-node_debug.lnk` → 5174). ⚠️ **A Chat hypothesis died on contact:** `--service` was suspected of starting a Vite; `run-node.ps1:37` routes it to `cargo run` and **starts none**. 📌 **5174 has ALREADY cost one session** to the wrong diagnosis *"an orphaned CLIENT vite"*. *A rule that holds for two of three scripts is not a rule.* **THE `-Launch` RIDER — RETIRED, NOT REPAIRED:** it set the env var WebView2 ≥136 **ignores** (D-104, **contradicting its own header 150 lines above**) and ran `bin\xgen-client.exe`, **the 21-MAY binary** — two reassuring lines + `exit 1` + a plausible XGen window. Runbook `tasks/M_RP_SELF_VARIANTS.md` v1.0 **COMPLETED**. **🔒 VERDICT — JOE, 2026-07-23, verbatim: *"v3 + 90% grey colour (90% white in grey)"*, confirmed as applied.** Values for `M-RP-SELF-SURFACE`: **`font-weight: 600` · `font-style: italic` · `letter-spacing: 0.05em` · `color: #E5E5E5`**, and ⚠️ **`font-synthesis: none` is REQUIRED or a skew silently satisfies the italic.** **🔑 THE FINDING THAT REFRAMES IT: the name was ALREADY `600`** ⇒ the distinction is carried by **italic + tracking + brightness, NOT by weight**; *"bold" was only ever one step (600→700), and 800/900 remain.* **⚠️ IT ALSO REPAIRED A DEFECT NOBODY WAS MEASURING:** the name sat at **2.65 : 1 at 10px — below WCAG AA** — and the chosen colour takes it to **14.1 : 1**; *the question asked was "which reads as Self", and the answer incidentally fixed a machine-checkable failure no test covers (**the J-568 shape: appearance has no verifier, so appearance defects survive**).* **THE CONTROL WAS THE REAL CONTENT (N-161):** `getComputedStyle().fontFamily` returns the **declared stack, not the resolved face**, and a browser **fakes italic by skewing Regular** ⇒ `font-synthesis: none` + advance-width vs a **bogus family**, after a forced `document.fonts.load()`. Measured: real **133.04** vs bogus **118.80** · italic **133.68 ≠ 133.04** ⇒ **a TRUE italic, not J-570's skew** · **600 → 135.95 with no 600 file on disk** ⇒ variable axis real. **🔒 JOE'S SCOPE RULINGS:** typeface is **not** the only axis (CSS styling first, mono family held in reserve) · **name only** · and the distinction applies **everywhere, not only where avatars gather** — in the thread it is merely *the only* change, because the avatar's side carries the rest. ⚠️ *Chat had proposed the message row as a **compromise** surface; Joe's reasoning made it a **legitimate** one.* **🔑 AND A RULING ON HOW QUESTIONS ARE PUT** — Joe: *"i cannot imagine font weight from number"* ⇒ **weights in words, numbers only in records**; *a question whose answer he cannot perceive is not a perception question.* **⚠️ THE LIMIT, RECORDED WITH THE VERDICT: there were NO inbound names on screen** ⇒ this proves the styling **reads as deliberate and legible**, NOT that it **DISTINGUISHES** — nothing was present to distinguish it from. *Closing it as "distinction achieved" would be the J-560 defect in a new place.* Needs **M-RP6.4 backfill or a second identity**. **📌 FILED, NOT FIXED:** ① the **self panel already resolves `display_name` ⇒ "Joe"** while message rows fall to the **65-char XGID** (`self-panel.svelte:43` vs `entity-item.svelte:65` / `message.svelte:69`) — same identity, two surfaces, two answers ② **`region-tile#region-members` is a stub with no panel** — the gathered-avatar surface **is not built** ③ **registry ladder deviation**: documented 149→**156**→158, measured **149→158 on space selection alone** — an eighth axis or an auto-latch, unexplained. → **`M-RP-SELF-SURFACE`** carries the values; ⚠️ **`M-RP-SELF-NAME`'s persisted `widgetId:"self"` migration is the harder gate.**

D-123 creates no trump card.** **⚠️ METHOD FINDING — CHAT HAD BEEN INJECTING CRLF INTO LF FILES ALL SESSION, VISIBLE ONLY VIA A NORMALISATION COMMIT:** `6708a71` carried three vite configs and `M_RP_LOCK_RECHECK.md` as changed **with no substantive edit** — dirty purely from line endings. **The repo is PER-FILE, not uniform** (JOURNAL **LF** · CLAUDE **CRLF** · ROADMAP **CRLF** · DECISIONS **LF** · both task docs **LF**) ⇒ **detect and match, never impose.** *A diff that reports a file changed when nothing changed hides the changes that matter.* **⚠️ TWO ROUNDS LOST TO AN UNRESPONSIVE MCP SERVER, one mid-write** — recovery followed the standing rule (check disk and git before assuming), found the work had landed and Joe had already pushed it, and **re-verified every recovered claim against the FILE, not the commit message.** **⚠️ M-RP-LOCK-RECHECK REMAINS ACTIVE — LEG C is the only thing between it and close; all twelve locks now have verdicts.**

> ### 🔒 **D-122 FINAL — "WINDOW" IS A LOOSE UMBRELLA; "MODAL AREA" AND "SEPARATE WINDOW" ARE THE FIXED TERMS; DISPLAY FORM IS DECIDED IN SITU (J-572). Records + vocabulary, ZERO code.** **🔑 THE CONTRADICTION HAD A CAUSE, AND IT WAS NOT CARELESSNESS.** Four locked docs said `window` = *"its own OS window"* while Joe's D-A (2026-07-16) said *standalone modal area*. The origin — **Ch6 §6.8.3, Session 2, April 2026** — reads *"a full separate desktop window … opened, minimised, and closed without affecting the main application window."* **Unambiguous; a modal area cannot be minimised. The text was never vague.** **🔑 THE MENTAL MODEL EXPLAINS IT:** Joe meant a Delphi modal subwindow *with Owner set* — **and a Delphi owned modal form IS a real OS window.** ***In that vocabulary "modal subwindow" and "OS window" were THE SAME OBJECT; the distinction we now draw (in-DOM overlay vs separate webview) was not expressible.*** ⇒ **"modal" names a BEHAVIOUR, "window" names a MECHANISM, and the project inherited a model where they were inseparable** (N-160). *One word carrying two properties fused in an older model splits silently the moment the newer model separates them — and every doc written in between looks correct. Ch6 was right, D-A was right, and they contradicted each other.* **🔒 JOE'S RESOLUTION WAS SMALLER THAN CHAT'S:** Chat proposed splitting the word across five locked docs; **Joe took `window`/`modal` LIGHTLY — a loose umbrella for "a new area with information not displayed before it opened" — with mechanism chosen PER SURFACE**, then named **`separate window`** and **`modal area`** as the fixed terms. **🔑 BOTH WERE ALREADY IN THE RECORDS** (*"standalone modal area"* = D-A; *"a full separate desktop window"* = Ch6's own April sentence) ⇒ ***adopting `separate window` does not correct Ch6, it makes Ch6 correct as written.*** **Nothing migrated except the vocabulary table.** **🔒 THE CLAUSE THAT MATTERS MOST — DISPLAY FORM IS DECIDED IN SITU, NEVER INHERITED:** *"in the moment, not from the old records. Minimum re-discuss it."* When the Auth plugin is built, **Ch6 is HISTORY, NOT INSTRUCTION.** 🔑 **Forced, not preferred: display form is a [👁️ PERCEPTION] call and those cannot be made from records** — proven twice the same day (lock #5 unfalsifiable until someone looked at a screen; three typeface variants void because the thing on screen was not the subject, J-570). ***You cannot look at a document.*** **⚠️ TWO GUARDS:** ① **reasoning is inherited even when the conclusion is not** (Ch6's *why* survives a flipped answer, else every re-discussion restarts from zero); ② **SCOPE = DISPLAY FORM ONLY** — for the wire, identity, the no-anonymity core, GDPR and anything irreversible **records remain BINDING**; *that sentence would be dangerous if it escaped its category.* **⚠️ D-122 NAMES MECHANISMS WITHOUT ASSIGNING THEM** and records **Joe's standing reservation to change any display form at any time** — ***stable words, mutable referents.*** **⚠️ CHAT HYGIENE DEFECT, SELF-CAUGHT:** Chat had been **injecting CRLF into predominantly-LF files all session** (46 in JOURNAL, 46 in DECISIONS, 23 in ui-notes, 7 per `vite.config.js` — the last already shipped in `9f427a0`). **The repo is PER-FILE, not uniform** (CLAUDE/ROADMAP/skin.css natively CRLF; Ch6, surfaces/tier/dock docs and configs pure LF). *Match the file; do not impose a convention.* All normalised. **Found only because an anchor guard failed and the diagnosis went one level past "retry".** **⚠️ STILL OPEN:** the **consistency rule** (*same mechanism unless a named reason*) · the **Auth Module's form** — Ch6 names it the first separate-window customer, but ⚠️ **a separate window is harder for a user to tie back to the app that spawned it, exactly what a credential-phishing surface would want; an undetachable modal area may be SAFER.** Identity-adjacent ⇒ Joe's, re-opened *in situ*. **M-RP-LOCK-RECHECK REMAINS ACTIVE — #11 has its verdict; LEG C is the only thing left.**

Joe: ***"if somewhere appears a sentence -smt opens window- I will always understand it as modal area"***, exceptions reserved ⇒ **#11 reads at VIEW scope and is MET.** **⚠️ THE PROJECT HAD ALREADY ANSWERED THIS TWICE, OPPOSITELY:** `window` is locked as *"its own OS window"* in **four** docs (`surfaces-phase0.md:132`, **W-12** at `:155`, `region-dock-model.md:24`, `widget-tier.md:96`) while **D-A (Joe, 2026-07-16)** already ruled it means *a standalone modal area*. **The drift predates this session; D-122 NAMES it and deliberately does NOT fix it** — reconciling a locked taxonomy across four docs is its own records arc. **🔑 THE BOUNDARY THAT WOULD HAVE BITTEN SILENTLY: #8 AND #11 CONTRADICT EACH OTHER AT OS-WINDOW SCOPE, AND NOBODY HAD PUT THEM SIDE BY SIDE.** Each webview is its own JS context ⇒ module `$state` is per-window, and **#8 makes the echo session-mortal and never persisted** ⇒ two OS windows = **two independent echo stores by construction**. *Unwritten, the likeliest outcome is a user opening a second view and watching a message they just sent simply not be there.* **🔑🔑 THEN THE REAL QUESTION — "TWO ROOMS SIDE BY SIDE?" — AND IT IS NOT A WINDOWING PROBLEM.** Two stream tiles today would render the **SAME room**: `stream-panel.svelte:37` takes `{ regionId, id }` with **NO room prop**, `:70` reads `roomLatch.effectiveRoomId`, `room-latch.svelte.ts:39` is **one `_latched`, one writer**. The dock already gives the geometry free; a second OS window gives the identical result plus an unshared context. ***The viewport was never the blocker — the binding is.*** **⚠️ AND THE GATE IS W-12, NOT CODE:** *"a widget MAY declare exactly one of: `region` … at most one"* ⇒ two stream regions violate the locked model, so this starts as a **taxonomy decision affecting every widget**, not an implementation task. **🔑 TWO THINGS MAKE IT CHEAPER THAN IT LOOKS:** ① `echo.forRoom(roomId)` is **already room-keyed** and would serve two rooms **unchanged** — only the view is a singleton; ② ***per-view binding is an unnamed prerequisite TWO FILED MILESTONES ALREADY SHARE*** — **M-RP-SELF-SURFACE** (Saved Messages) and **M11/D-021** (the `self` thread) are each *a stream bound to something other than the latched room*. Nobody had connected them. **🔒 Multi-window DEFERRED, not refused:** the real case is a second monitor (a modal cannot serve it), but it is not the blocker for what was asked and additionally needs a second Vite entry, a second CDP target, typed Rust geometry (D-114/D-115) and a shared-state mechanism that does not exist. ***Nothing is wasted by waiting — the binding work is a prerequisite for the window case anyway.*** **⚠️ RECORDED OPEN ON JOE'S INSTRUCTION:** `tasks/M_RP_VIEW_BINDING.md` **PENDING v0.1**, **five questions, NONE answered** — W-12 relax vs widget *instances* · does the latch become "the active room" · what a pinned tile shows when its room disappears · **does the composer follow the latch or the focused tile (⚠️ sending into the wrong room is identity-attributed and permanent — the highest-risk question)** · per-tile unread. *Recording a discussion mid-thought is the point: the alternative is re-deriving it from scratch, which already happened to this one.* D-122 · N-159. **⚠️ M-RP-LOCK-RECHECK REMAINS ACTIVE — #11 now has its verdict, so LEG C is the only thing between it and close.**

> ### ✅ **M-RP-FONTS — DECLARE THE BUNDLED TYPE, AND THE 403 THAT HID IT — DONE (J-570). Code + assets; NO Rust (cargo identical, which proves it).** **🔑 SCOPED AS A TYPOGRAPHY CHORE; FOUND A DEFECT OLDER THAN THE MILESTONE.** `skin.css` asked for weights 500/600/700 at **fourteen** sites and italic at **two** while declaring **ONE** face (Inter-Regular 400) — every bold and italic was a synthesised smear. Fixing that meant declaring the rest; then the verify leg asked what nobody had asked: **do the fonts actually load? They did not — not the new ones, and NOT the Inter-Regular declared all along.** **⚠️ ROOT CAUSE:** `ui/assets/` sits **outside** each app's Vite root, so `skin.css`'s `url()` resolves to `/@fs/` and the dev server answers **403**. The stylesheet loads fine because it arrives through `$assets` as a **module, not a served file** — ***so the CSS was correct, the files existed, and nothing ever fetched them.*** The dev client had rendered **`system-ui`/Segoe UI** for the project's whole life. **🔑 WORSE THAN BROKEN, BECAUSE `vite build` EMITS THEM CORRECTLY** (4 fingerprinted assets verified in `client-dist`) ⇒ ***dev and the built app rendered in DIFFERENT TYPEFACES, and every appearance judgement in this project is made in dev.*** A uniform failure would have been caught years ago; one that only affects the surface people look at is invisible by construction. **🔑🔑 AND THE FIX WAS ALREADY IN THE REPO, WITH ITS REASONING.** `ui/sampler/vitest.config.js` carries it verbatim from **J-491** — *"the suites live outside this package's root, so widen Vite's fs allow-list to the repo root."* **Same constraint, same layout, same solution — applied to the TEST HARNESS and never generalised to the three DEV SERVERS.** ***A lesson filed against one consumer is not a lesson the project has learned.*** → `server.fs.allow` added to **client, node AND sampler**. **🔒 JOE OVERTURNED CHAT'S FIRST ANSWER.** Chat recommended **all 34 statics** (~4 MB) and flagged variable fonts as *"worth ten minutes before committing"*; Joe pointed at the upstream distros already in `fonts/` — **both families ship VARIABLE builds** → **4 files, ~1.35 MB** (`InterVariable` +italic **100–900**, `JetBrainsMono` +italic **100–800**). *The ten minutes Chat flagged and did not spend were the ten that mattered.* **🔑 THE DECIDING ARGUMENT WAS NOT SIZE, IT WAS THE TRAP:** a static subset means the next undeclared `font-weight` falls back to a smear **silently, no error, no failing test**; a continuous axis makes every weight real. ***The person a silent type fallback catches is whoever is tuning appearance*** — and `skin.css` is Joe's, tuned live over HMR. *J-568's finding again: appearance has no automated verifier, and type had none either — which is exactly why it stayed broken.* **⚠️ DIRECT COST TO WORK ALREADY DONE: Joe's three "Self" typeface variants were ALL FALLBACK FONTS** — no JetBrains Mono face was ever declared, so variant 2 could not have been it, and variant 3's italic was a skewed Regular. ***A [👁️ PERCEPTION] verdict is only as good as whether the thing looked at was the subject*** — filed to be RE-RUN, not salvaged (N-157 at the type layer). **VERIFIED, with the control that made it mean anything:** ⚠️ `document.fonts.check()` returns **false for an UNLOADED face**, so *"never requested"* and *"broken"* return the same string → every check preceded by a forced `document.fonts.load()`. Fetch **403 → 200** · all four faces **`loaded`** · `check('600 …')` and `check('italic 700 … Mono')` **true** — **combinations with NO static file on disk, so the variable axis is proven not assumed** · the head-marker notice now computes italic against a **loaded** italic face. Registry **149** at open, **149 → 156 → 158** on latch. **FLOORS:** cargo **1553/0/62 across 56 — IDENTICAL** (proves no Rust) · vite **202/170** · npm **154** · svelte-check **0/34/15** · catalogue **419**. **⚠️ The `ui/templates` Inter copy was deliberately KEPT** (referenced by its own `app.css`) — Chat's earlier *"three stale copies"* was **two of three**, corrected by grounding **before** anything was deleted. **⚠️ TWO OF CHAT'S OWN MISTAKES, BOTH CAUGHT BY GUARDS:** ① **`Copy-Item` copied NOTHING and reported success** — `[wght]` in the filename is a PowerShell **wildcard character class**; `-LiteralPath` fixes it (*N-156's shape in the shell*). ② **a `Regex.Replace` escape corrupted `skin.css` into one escaped line** — caught by **reading the file back rather than trusting the `@font-face` count**, reverted after confirming only that file was dirty. A third guard wrote nothing when a here-string's LF met a CRLF file. ***All three were caught by a read-back or an anchor assertion, which is the only reason none shipped.*** **OWED:** M-RP-FONTS-WOFF2 (mono TTF→woff2, size not correctness) · M-RP-SELF-VARIANTS (re-run the perception test). `tasks/M_RP_FONTS.md` v1.0. **No new D.** ⚠️ **M-RP-LOCK-RECHECK REMAINS ACTIVE — #11's wording and Leg C still owed; none of this touched them.**

**zero S1 configs exist here**, and since no config could carry a stale seed until Leg C shipped yesterday, **the migration's real population today is ONE MACHINE** — *the bound still matters as precedent; saying the urgency is lower is cheaper than discovering it mid-build.* **THE BOUND (D-100 ②), WRITTEN BEFORE THE CODE:** byte-identity only — never a substring match, prefix/suffix test, normalisation, trim, parse-and-compare or heuristic · an explicit list of **named constants**, the old seed kept **because the constant IS the evidence** · **a curated list is NEVER migrated** · the list never grows silently. **And the mechanism is smaller than the ceremony: the migration is a SKIP, not a rewrite** — `write_fresh_config` has already written the new seed, so migrating means *not overwriting it back*. **⚠️ V-A2 IS THE LEG THIS MILESTONE EXISTS FOR AND §5 NAMES IT BY NAME:** every pair typed **one character at a time**, **positive control** (a non-rule token comes through unmorphed) and **negative control** (`-->` must now yield `‒>`, proving the relation is gone rather than hidden) — *a wholesale value-set verifies the transform function, not the feature, and that is exactly how this shipped broken* (N-154). **⚠️ LEGS A+B MUST NOT BE SPLIT** — the seed without the migration fixes nobody who already has the bug. **Apps and dev servers stopped, verified BY PORT** (all seven 0 listeners). **No new D-number, no new N.** **🟢 NEXT-ACTIVE = M-RP-PROCESSOR-SEED implementation from runbook v1.0 (Clair).** → JOURNAL J-566 · DECISIONS D-100 second amendment · ROADMAP v5.32 · `tasks/M_RP_PROCESSOR_SEED.md` v1.0.

NO schema change** (a split id would be a key nothing round-trips — M-RP6.1k); **M-RP7.4's `move` needs the identical addressing, so it is paid once.** **L6 — the min-clamp READS THE SKIN** (N-090); **it stops, it never auto-folds** (§4.2). **L7 — NO keyboard resize** (a tab stop on all **7** seams nobody asked for, *and* it would dodge the harness this milestone exists to land). **L8 — THE SEAM DOES NOT REGISTER** (`.region-split` has no getter either — the seam is its chrome): **registry stays 67**; proof is the tree diff + painted geometry. **⚠️ TWO RETRACTIONS, BOTH MINE:** Chat **over-asked** — three questions to Joe, **two of them Chat's own to answer** under §0 (*autonomy is not a licence to be vague; it is an obligation to decide*) — and Chat **named a scrollbar-collision risk WITHOUT MEASURING IT** (rule ③ cuts both ways: **do not name a risk you have not grounded**).  

> **🎯 M-RP-FOCUS — FOCUS IS A STATE, NOT A BRAND (filed 2026-07-12, Joe-locked; PENDING, skin-only, gates nothing).** **The library focuses two ways today and one is wrong.** ✅ The **field family** (`textfield`/`textarea`/`number`/`date`/`select`/`select-multiple`/`color`/`converter-field`) already indicates focus with a **lighter-grey BORDER-LIFT** (`border-color: var(--t3)`, `outline:none`) — correct: quiet, legible. ❌ The **action/nav family — ~13 components** (`button`·`link`·`toggle`·`range`·`file`·`star-rating`·`file-field`·`section-toggle`·`cp-num`·`entity-panel-option`·`entity-context-menu`·`menu-trigger`·`menu-item`) still rides `--focus-ring`, a glow **hardcoded to `--accent2`**. **🔑 THE ROOT CAUSE IS A MISSING NAME:** there is **no `--focus` token** — the ring hardcodes the accent, and the correct field fix borrows **`--t3`, a TEXT-RAMP token** (so it currently means *"dim text"*, not *"focused"*). **A token's NAME is what stops the next person re-pointing it at the accent.** **Fix:** add **`--focus`** as its own semantic token (light grey; **NOT an alias of `--t3`/`--t4`, NOT the accent**) → point `--focus-ring` at it → **one line flips all ~13 off the accent with ZERO component edits**; field family takes `border-color: var(--focus)`. **Open at kickoff:** glow vs border-lift for the action family (*one treatment library-wide is cleaner — they have borders too*). **Supersedes “Q5 focus policy: accent” (J-412).** **Cost: `skin.css` ONLY** — no component code, no registry change (*the N-025/N-090 payoff made literal*). **⚠️ KNOCK-ON:** milestones asserting *“the focus ring is the ONLY accent carrier”* (menu-bar·status-bar·separator·M-RP6.1i) become *“nothing moves under an accent swap”* — **update the assertion; it is NOT a regression.**
